```
## CHECKPOINT REACHED

**Type:** human-verify
**Debug Session:** .planning/debug/google-oauth-tauri-redirect.md
**Progress:** 6 evidence entries, 0 hypotheses eliminated

### Investigation State

**Current Hypothesis:** CONFIRMED -- Google OAuth rejects `tauri://` scheme redirect URIs because it only accepts `http://` and `https://` schemes. In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to `tauri://localhost` (macOS), causing `redirect_uri=tauri://localhost/google-callback.html` which Google rejects with Error 400.

**Evidence Summary:**
- `auth.ts` line 703 builds redirect_uri from `window.location.origin`, which is `tauri://localhost` in production builds
- Google OAuth only accepts `http://` and `https://` redirect URIs
- The web app avoids this issue entirely by using Google Identity Services (GIS) One Tap, which doesn't need a redirect URI

### Root Cause

`apps/desktop/src/auth.ts` line 703: `const redirectUri = \`${window.location.origin}/google-callback.html\`;`

In production builds, `window.location.origin` = `tauri://localhost`, producing a redirect URI that Google rejects.

### Fix Applied

Three files changed:

1. **`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/oauth.rs`** -- Added `start_oauth_server` command that spins up a temporary localhost HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`. Tries preferred ports 14200/14201/14202 first (so they can be pre-registered in Google Cloud Console), falls back to random. The server serves a callback HTML page that reads the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back. On receiving the POST, it emits a Tauri `oauth-callback` event and shuts down.

2. **`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs`** -- Registered the new `start_oauth_server` command in both debug and release invoke handlers.

3. **`apps/desktop/src/auth.ts`** -- Rewrote `getGoogleCredential()` to call `start_oauth_server` first to get a port, use `http://localhost:PORT/callback` as the redirect URI, and listen for the `oauth-callback` Tauri event (instead of polling localStorage).

### Self-verified checks

- Rust code compiles cleanly (`cargo check -p cipherbox-desktop` -- zero errors)
- TypeScript compiles cleanly (`tsc --noEmit` -- zero errors)
- No new dependencies added (uses existing `tokio`, `serde_json`, Tauri events)
- CSRF validation preserved (state parameter is validated in the event handler)
- Server auto-terminates after 120s timeout
- OAuth popup windows auto-closed after callback received

### Google Cloud Console Setup Required

Before testing, add these authorized redirect URIs to the Google OAuth client (`164556913424-uif44is1apnl7rqc9a2ac7vn8u9dq0ee.apps.googleusercontent.com`) in Google Cloud Console:

- `http://localhost:14200/callback`
- `http://localhost:14201/callback`
- `http://localhost:14202/callback`

### How to verify

1. Add the redirect URIs to Google Cloud Console (see above)
2. Ensure `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` is set in `apps/desktop/.env`
3. Run the desktop app: `pnpm --filter desktop dev`
4. Click the Google login button in the tray menu login window
5. The Google OAuth consent page should appear in a popup
6. After signing in, the popup should close and login should complete

**Tell me:** "confirmed fixed" OR what's still failing (error messages, where it fails in the flow)
```

```
1	//! OAuth popup window and callback server commands.
2	
3	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
4	use tauri::{Emitter, Manager};
5	use tokio::io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncWriteExt};
6	use tokio::net::TcpListener;
7	
8	/// Counter for unique OAuth popup window labels (shared with tray handler).
9	static POPUP_COUNTER: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
10	
11	/// Open an OAuth popup window directly from Rust.
12	///
13	/// Bypasses `window.open()` which is unreliable on Windows WebView2 (the
14	/// `NewWindowRequested` event / `on_new_window` handler may silently fail).
15	/// Instead, the webview calls this command via `invoke()` to create a new
16	/// Tauri webview window pointing directly at the OAuth URL.
17	#[tauri::command]
18	pub async fn open_oauth_popup(app: tauri::AppHandle, url: String) -> Result<(), String> {
19	    let n = POPUP_COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
20	    let label = format!("oauth-popup-{}", n);
21	
22	    let parsed_url: tauri::Url = url
23	        .parse()
24	        .map_err(|e| format!("Invalid OAuth URL: {}", e))?;
25	
26	    // Allowlist: only HTTPS requests to known OAuth providers are permitted.
27	    const ALLOWED_HOSTS: &[&str] = &["accounts.google.com"];
28	    if parsed_url.scheme() != "https" {
29	        return Err("OAuth URL must use HTTPS".to_string());
30	    }
31	    let host = parsed_url.host_str().unwrap_or("");
32	    if !ALLOWED_HOSTS.contains(&host) {
33	        return Err(format!("OAuth URL host '{}' is not allowed", host));
34	    }
35	
36	    log::info!("Creating OAuth popup window: {} -> {}", label, host);
37	
38	    tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::new(
39	        &app,
40	        &label,
41	        tauri::WebviewUrl::External(parsed_url),
42	    )
43	    .title("Sign in with Google")
44	    .inner_size(500.0, 700.0)
45	    .center()
46	    .build()
47	    .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create OAuth popup: {}", e))?;
48	
49	    Ok(())
50	}
51	
52	/// Preferred ports for the OAuth callback server, tried in order.
53	///
54	/// These must be registered as authorized redirect URIs in Google Cloud Console:
55	///   - `http://localhost:14200/callback`
56	///   - `http://localhost:14201/callback`
57	///   - `http://localhost:14202/callback`
58	///
59	/// If none of the preferred ports are available, falls back to a random port.
60	/// A random port requires `http://localhost` (without port) to be registered
61	/// in Google Cloud Console, which Google allows for Desktop-type OAuth clients.
62	const PREFERRED_PORTS: &[u16] = &[14200, 14201, 14202];
63	
64	/// Start a temporary localhost HTTP server to receive OAuth callbacks.
65	///
66	/// Google OAuth rejects non-HTTP(S) redirect URIs like `tauri://localhost`.
67	/// In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to
68	/// `tauri://localhost` (macOS) or `https://tauri.localhost` (Windows),
69	/// which Google won't accept.
70	///
71	/// This command spins up a short-lived HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`,
72	/// trying preferred ports first (so the redirect URI can be pre-registered
73	/// in Google Cloud Console). The server:
74	///   1. Serves a callback HTML page at any GET request that extracts
75	///      the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back
76	///   2. Receives the POST at `/token`, emits a Tauri event, and shuts down
77	///
78	/// Returns the port number so the frontend can build the redirect_uri:
79	///   `http://localhost:{port}/callback`
80	///
81	/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds if no callback arrives.
82	#[tauri::command]
83	pub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<u16, String> {
84	    // Try preferred ports first (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)
85	    let mut listener = None;
86	    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {
87	        match TcpListener::bind(format!("127.0.0.1:{}", port)).await {
88	            Ok(l) => {
89	                log::info!("OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}", port);
90	                listener = Some(l);
91	                break;
92	            }
93	            Err(e) => {
94	                log::debug!("Preferred port {} unavailable: {}", port, e);
95	            }
96	        }
97	    }
98	
99	    // Fallback: random port
100	    let listener = match listener {
101	        Some(l) => l,
102	        None => {
103	            log::warn!(
104	                "All preferred ports {:?} unavailable, using random port",
105	                PREFERRED_PORTS
106	            );
107	            TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
108	                .await
109	                .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to bind OAuth callback server: {}", e))?
110	        }
111	    };
112	
113	    let port = listener
114	        .local_addr()
115	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to get server address: {}", e))?
116	        .port();
117	
118	    log::info!("OAuth callback server started on port {}", port);
119	
120	    // Spawn the server task — it will shut down after receiving the callback
121	    // or after the timeout (120s).
122	    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app));
123	
124	    Ok(port)
125	}
126	
127	/// Minimal callback HTML page served by the OAuth callback server.
128	///
129	/// This page runs in the OAuth popup webview after Google redirects back.
130	/// It extracts the token from the URL fragment (implicit flow uses fragments)
131	/// and POSTs it back to the same localhost server, which then emits a Tauri event.
132	const CALLBACK_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
133	<html>
134	<head>
135	<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
136	</head>
137	<body>
138	<p id="msg">Completing sign-in...</p>
139	<script>
140	(function() {
141	  var hash = window.location.hash.substring(1);
142	  var params = new URLSearchParams(hash);
143	  var idToken = params.get('id_token');
144	  var error = params.get('error');
145	  var state = params.get('state');
146	
147	  // POST the extracted fragment data back to the same server
148	  fetch('/token', {
149	    method: 'POST',
150	    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
151	    body: JSON.stringify({
152	      id_token: idToken || null,
153	      error: error || null,
154	      state: state || null
155	    })
156	  })
157	  .then(function() {
158	    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Sign-in complete. You can close this window.';
159	  })
160	  .catch(function(err) {
161	    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;
162	  });
163	})();
164	</script>
165	</body>
166	</html>"#;
167	
168	/// Response page shown after the token POST is received.
169	const DONE_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
170	<html>
171	<head>
172	<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
173	</head>
174	<body>
175	<p>Sign-in complete. You can close this window.</p>
176	</body>
177	</html>"#;
178	
179	/// OAuth callback data emitted via Tauri event.
180	#[derive(Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
181	struct OAuthCallbackPayload {
182	    id_token: Option<String>,
183	    error: Option<String>,
184	    state: Option<String>,
185	}
186	
187	/// Run the OAuth callback server until a token is received or timeout.
188	async fn run_oauth_server(listener: TcpListener, app: tauri::AppHandle) {
189	    let timeout = tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);
190	
191	    loop {
192	        let accept_result = tokio::select! {
193	            result = listener.accept() => result,
194	            _ = tokio::time::sleep(timeout) => {
195	                log::warn!("OAuth callback server timed out after 120s");
196	                // Emit error event so the frontend doesn't hang
197	                let _ = app.emit("oauth-callback", OAuthCallbackPayload {
198	                    id_token: None,
199	                    error: Some("OAuth callback server timed out".to_string()),
200	                    state: None,
201	                });
202	                return;
203	            }
204	        };
205	
206	        let (mut stream, _addr) = match accept_result {
207	            Ok(conn) => conn,
208	            Err(e) => {
209	                log::error!("OAuth server accept error: {}", e);
210	                continue;
211	            }
212	        };
213	
214	        // Read the HTTP request (up to 64KB should be more than enough)
215	        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];
216	        let n = match stream.read(&mut buf).await {
217	            Ok(n) if n > 0 => n,
218	            _ => continue,
219	        };
220	        let request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..n]);
221	
222	        // Parse the first line to determine method and path
223	        let first_line = request.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
224	
225	        if first_line.starts_with("POST /token") {
226	            // Extract JSON body from the request
227	            let body = request
228	                .split("\r\n\r\n")
229	                .nth(1)
230	                .unwrap_or("");
231	
232	            let payload: OAuthCallbackPayload =
233	                serde_json::from_str(body).unwrap_or(OAuthCallbackPayload {
234	                    id_token: None,
235	                    error: Some("Failed to parse callback data".to_string()),
236	                    state: None,
237	                });
238	
239	            let has_token = payload.id_token.is_some();
240	            log::info!(
241	                "OAuth callback received: has_token={}, error={:?}",
242	                has_token,
243	                payload.error
244	            );
245	
246	            // Emit the Tauri event to the main webview
247	            if let Err(e) = app.emit("oauth-callback", payload) {
248	                log::error!("Failed to emit oauth-callback event: {}", e);
249	            }
250	
251	            // Send response and close
252	            let response = format!(
253	                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
254	                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
255	                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
256	                 Connection: close\r\n\
257	                 Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
258	                 \r\n\
259	                 {}",
260	                DONE_HTML.len(),
261	                DONE_HTML
262	            );
263	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
264	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
265	
266	            // Also close any remaining oauth-popup windows
267	            close_oauth_popups(&app);
268	
269	            log::info!("OAuth callback server shutting down");
270	            return;
271	        } else if first_line.starts_with("OPTIONS") {
272	            // Handle CORS preflight for the POST
273	            let response = "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
274	                            Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
275	                            Access-Control-Allow-Methods: POST\r\n\
276	                            Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type\r\n\
277	                            Content-Length: 0\r\n\
278	                            Connection: close\r\n\
279	                            \r\n";
280	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
281	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
282	        } else {
283	            // Any other GET request (the callback page)
284	            let response = format!(
285	                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
286	                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
287	                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
288	                 Connection: close\r\n\
289	                 \r\n\
290	                 {}",
291	                CALLBACK_HTML.len(),
292	                CALLBACK_HTML
293	            );
294	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
295	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
296	        }
297	    }
298	}
299	
300	/// Close all oauth-popup windows.
301	fn close_oauth_popups(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {
302	    // Popup labels are "oauth-popup-0", "oauth-popup-1", etc.
303	    // Try to close a reasonable range.
304	    for i in 0..100 {
305	        let label = format!("oauth-popup-{}", i);
306	        if let Some(window) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {
307	            let _ = window.close();
308	        }
309	    }
310	}
```

```
16	/// Check if WinFsp filesystem driver is installed on Windows.
17	///
18	/// Queries the Windows Registry (HKLM\SOFTWARE\WinFsp) for the install directory
19	/// and verifies the winfsp-x64.dll exists at the expected path.
20	/// Returns true if WinFsp is installed and the DLL is present.
21	#[cfg(target_os = "windows")]
22	fn check_winfsp_installed() -> bool {
23	    use winreg::enums::*;
24	    use winreg::RegKey;
25	    let hklm = RegKey::predef(HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE);
26	    // WinFsp is a 32-bit installer, so on 64-bit Windows the registry key
27	    // lives under WOW6432Node. Try the native path first, then WOW6432Node.
28	    let subkeys = [
29	        "SOFTWARE\\WinFsp",
30	        "SOFTWARE\\WOW6432Node\\WinFsp",
31	    ];
32	    for subkey in &subkeys {
33	        if let Ok(key) = hklm.open_subkey(subkey) {
34	            if let Ok(dir) = key.get_value::<String, _>("InstallDir") {
35	                let dll_path = std::path::Path::new(&dir).join("bin").join("winfsp-x64.dll");
36	                if dll_path.exists() {
37	                    return true;
38	                }
39	            }
40	        }
41	    }
42	    false
43	}

85	    // 4. Fallback to localhost (pure local dev)

87	        .or_else(|_| std::env::var("VITE_API_URL"))

105	        .plugin(tauri_plugin_notification::init())
106	        .plugin(tauri_plugin_updater::Builder::new().build())
107	        .manage(app_state)
108	        .setup(|app| {
109	            // Hide dock icon -- menu bar only (pure background utility)
110	            #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
111	            app.set_activation_policy(tauri::ActivationPolicy::Accessory);
```

```
1	/**
2	 * CipherBox Desktop - Webview Auth Module (Core Kit)
3	 *
4	 * Authenticates via CipherBox identity provider (Google OAuth, Email OTP),
5	 * then calls Core Kit loginWithJWT to derive the TSS key. After Core Kit
6	 * authentication, the CipherBox JWT and private key are passed to the
7	 * Rust backend via Tauri IPC commands (secure in-process channel).
8	 *
9	 * The private key never leaves the process -- it stays within the webview
10	 * and is passed to Rust via invoke(), not URL parameters or deep links.
11	 *
12	 * Auth flow:
13	 *   1. User authenticates via CipherBox backend (Google/Email)
14	 *   2. Backend issues a CipherBox JWT (RS256, iss=cipherbox, aud=web3auth)
15	 *   3. Frontend calls Core Kit loginWithJWT with the CipherBox JWT
16	 *   4. Core Kit derives TSS key -> _UNSAFE_exportTssKey -> private key hex
17	 *   5. Private key + CipherBox JWT passed to Rust via invoke('handle_auth_complete')
18	 *
19	 * MFA flow (REQUIRED_SHARE):
20	 *   When Core Kit returns REQUIRED_SHARE, the user needs a second factor.
21	 *   Two paths are supported:
22	 *   a) Recovery phrase: mnemonic -> mnemonicToKey -> inputFactorKey -> login complete
23	 *   b) Device approval: ephemeral keypair -> bulletin board request -> poll -> ECIES decrypt -> login complete
24	 */

49	const WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID = import.meta.env.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID || '';

53

55	  local: WEB3AUTH_NETWORK.DEVNET,
56	  ci: WEB3AUTH_NETWORK.DEVNET,
57	  staging: WEB3AUTH_NETWORK.DEVNET,

62	
63	// Module-level state for MFA flow

68	export type LoginResult = { status: 'logged_in' } | { status: 'required_share' };

70	/**
71	 * Initialize Core Kit singleton.
72	 * Must be called once before any login flow.
73	 * Matches the web app configuration exactly (same verifier, same network).
74	 */
75	export async function initCoreKit(): Promise<void> {
76	  if (coreKit) return;
77	
78	  coreKit = new Web3AuthMPCCoreKit({
79	    web3AuthClientId: WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID,
80	    web3AuthNetwork: NETWORK_MAP[environment] || WEB3AUTH_NETWORK.DEVNET,
81	    storage: window.localStorage,

97	/**
98	 * Login with Google via CipherBox identity provider.
99	 *
100	 * Flow:
101	 * 1. Load Google Identity Services (GIS) script in webview
102	 * 2. Get Google credential (JWT) via GIS prompt
103	 * 3. Send Google credential to CipherBox backend -> CipherBox JWT
104	 * 4. Core Kit loginWithJWT with CipherBox JWT
105	 * 5. Export TSS key and pass to Rust backend
106	 */
107	export async function loginWithGoogle(): Promise<LoginResult> {
108	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
109	
110	  // 1. Get Google credential via Google Identity Services
111	  const googleIdToken = await getGoogleCredential();
112	
113	  // 2. Send Google credential to CipherBox backend identity endpoint
114	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/identity/google`, {
115	    method: 'POST',
116	    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
117	    body: JSON.stringify({ idToken: googleIdToken }),
118	  });
119	  if (!resp.ok) {
120	    const body = await resp.text().catch(() => '');
121	    throw new Error(`Google auth failed (${resp.status}): ${body}`);
122	  }
123	  const { idToken: cipherboxJwt, userId } = await resp.json();
124	
125	  // 3. Core Kit login + key export
126	  return await loginWithCoreKit(cipherboxJwt, userId);
127	}

132	 */
133	export async function requestEmailOtp(email: string): Promise<void> {
134	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/identity/email/send-otp`, {
135	    method: 'POST',
136	    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
137	    body: JSON.stringify({ email: email.toLowerCase().trim() }),
138	  });
139	  if (!resp.ok) {
140	    if (resp.status === 429) {
141	      throw new Error('Too many attempts. Please wait before trying again.');
142	    }
143	    throw new Error('Failed to send verification code');
144	  }
145	}

147	/**

149	 *

152	 * 2. Core Kit loginWithJWT with CipherBox JWT
153	 * 3. Export TSS key and pass to Rust backend
154	 */
155	export async function loginWithEmailOtp(email: string, otp: string): Promise<LoginResult> {
156	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
157	
158	  // 1. Verify OTP with CipherBox backend
159	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/identity/email/verify-otp`, {
160	    method: 'POST',

162	    body: JSON.stringify({ email: email.toLowerCase().trim(), otp }),
163	  });
164	  if (!resp.ok) {
165	    if (resp.status === 401) {
166	      throw new Error('Invalid code. Please check and try again.');
167	    }
168	    throw new Error('OTP verification failed');
169	  }
170	  const { idToken: cipherboxJwt, userId } = await resp.json();
171	
172	  // 2. Core Kit login + key export

176	/**
177	 * Login with an Ethereum wallet via SIWE (Sign-In with Ethereum).
178	 *
179	 * Flow:
180	 * 1. Detect injected wallet provider (window.ethereum)
181	 * 2. Request accounts via eth_requestAccounts
182	 * 3. Fetch SIWE nonce from CipherBox API
183	 * 4. Build EIP-4361 SIWE message (matching web app format exactly)
184	 * 5. Sign with personal_sign
185	 * 6. Verify signature on CipherBox API -> CipherBox JWT
186	 * 7. Core Kit loginWithJWT (same as Google/Email)
187	 *
188	 * Note: Tauri webview may not support browser wallet extensions.
189	 * If window.ethereum is not available, an informative error is shown.
190	 * WalletConnect QR code flow is deferred to Phase 11 (cross-platform).
191	 */
192	export async function loginWithWallet(): Promise<LoginResult> {
193	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
194	
195	  // 1. Check for injected wallet provider
196	  const ethereum = (window as unknown as { ethereum?: EthereumProvider }).ethereum;
197	  if (!ethereum) {
198	    throw new Error(
199	      'No Ethereum wallet detected. Install a wallet browser extension to use wallet login.'
200	    );
201	  }
202	
203	  // 2. Request accounts
204	  const accounts = (await ethereum.request({ method: 'eth_requestAccounts' })) as string[];
205	  if (!accounts || accounts.length === 0) {
206	    throw new Error('No wallet accounts available');
207	  }
208	  const address = accounts[0];
209	
210	  // 3. Get SIWE nonce from CipherBox API
211	  const nonceResp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/identity/wallet/nonce`);
212	  if (!nonceResp.ok) throw new Error('Failed to get SIWE nonce');
213	  const { nonce } = await nonceResp.json();
214	
215	  // 4. Create SIWE message (EIP-4361 format matching web app's createSiweMessage)
216	  const domain = new URL(API_BASE).host;
217	  const uri = API_BASE;
218	  const issuedAt = new Date().toISOString();
219	  const siweMessage = [
220	    `${domain} wants you to sign in with your Ethereum account:`,
221	    address,
222	    '',
223	    'Sign in to CipherBox encrypted storage',
224	    '',
225	    `URI: ${uri}`,
226	    `Version: 1`,
227	    `Chain ID: 1`,
228	    `Nonce: ${nonce}`,
229	    `Issued At: ${issuedAt}`,
230	  ].join('\n');
231	
232	  // 5. Sign SIWE message with wallet
233	  const signature = (await ethereum.request({
234	    method: 'personal_sign',
235	    params: [siweMessage, address],
236	  })) as string;
237	
238	  // 6. Verify signature with CipherBox API
239	  const verifyResp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/identity/wallet`, {
240	    method: 'POST',
241	    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
242	    body: JSON.stringify({ message: siweMessage, signature }),
243	  });
244	  if (!verifyResp.ok) {
245	    const body = await verifyResp.text().catch(() => '');
246	    throw new Error(`SIWE verification failed (${verifyResp.status}): ${body}`);
247	  }
248	  const { idToken: cipherboxJwt, userId } = await verifyResp.json();
249	
250	  // 7. Core Kit loginWithJWT (same as Google/Email)
251	  return await loginWithCoreKit(cipherboxJwt, userId);
252	}

254	/**
255	 * Core Kit loginWithJWT + TSS key export + Rust handoff.
256	 *
257	 * Uses the exact same verifier name ('cipherbox-identity') and parameters
258	 * as the web app to ensure the same TSS key is derived for the same user.
259	 *
260	 * On REQUIRED_SHARE: stores the JWT and obtains a temporary access token
261	 * from the backend so the MFA UI can call device-approval endpoints.
262	 */
263	async function loginWithCoreKit(cipherboxJwt: string, userId: string): Promise<LoginResult> {
264	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
265	
266	  // Store JWT for MFA completion later
267	  lastCipherboxJwt = cipherboxJwt;
268	
269	  // loginWithJWT with CipherBox identity verifier (matches web app exactly)
270	  console.log('[CoreKit] loginWithJWT starting...', {
271	    verifier: 'cipherbox-identity',
272	    verifierId: userId,
273	  });
274	  await coreKit.loginWithJWT({
275	    verifier: 'cipherbox-identity',
276	    verifierId: userId,
277	    idToken: cipherboxJwt,
278	  });
279	  console.log('[CoreKit] loginWithJWT completed, status:', coreKit.status);
280	
281	  // Handle REQUIRED_SHARE status (MFA enabled, device factor missing)
282	  if (coreKit.status === COREKIT_STATUS.REQUIRED_SHARE) {
283	    console.log('[CoreKit] REQUIRED_SHARE -- MFA challenge needed');
284	
285	    // Obtain temporary backend access token so the device can call
286	    // bulletin board API endpoints (device-approval/*).
287	    // Uses placeholder publicKey since Core Kit is in REQUIRED_SHARE
288	    // state and we can't export the TSS key yet.
289	    try {
290	      const tempResp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/auth/login`, {
291	        method: 'POST',
292	        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
293	        body: JSON.stringify({
294	          idToken: cipherboxJwt,
295	          publicKey: `pending-core-kit-${userId}`,
296	          loginType: 'corekit',
297	        }),
298	      });
299	      if (tempResp.ok) {
300	        const { accessToken } = await tempResp.json();
301	        temporaryAccessToken = accessToken;
302	        console.log('[CoreKit] Temporary access token obtained for MFA flow');
303	      } else {
304	        console.warn('[CoreKit] Failed to get temporary token:', tempResp.status);
305	      }
306	    } catch (err) {
307	      console.warn('[CoreKit] Temporary token request failed:', err);
308	    }
309	
310	    return { status: 'required_share' };
311	  }
312	
313	  if (coreKit.status !== COREKIT_STATUS.LOGGED_IN) {
314	    throw new Error(`Unexpected Core Kit status: ${coreKit.status}`);
315	  }
316	
317	  // Commit changes (persist device factor to Web3Auth network)
318	  try {
319	    await coreKit.commitChanges();
320	    console.log('[CoreKit] commitChanges done');
321	  } catch (err) {
322	    console.error('[CoreKit] commitChanges failed:', err);
323	  }
324	
325	  // Export TSS private key and complete auth
326	  await completeAuthHandoff(cipherboxJwt);
327	
328	  return { status: 'logged_in' };
329	}
330	
331	/**
332	 * Export TSS key and pass credentials to Rust backend via Tauri IPC.
333	 * Shared between normal login and MFA recovery completion.
334	 */
335	async function completeAuthHandoff(cipherboxJwt: string): Promise<void> {
336	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
337	
338	  console.log('[CoreKit] Exporting TSS key...');
339	  const privateKeyHex = await coreKit._UNSAFE_exportTssKey();
340	  const privKeyHex = privateKeyHex.startsWith('0x') ? privateKeyHex.slice(2) : privateKeyHex;
341	  console.log('[CoreKit] TSS key exported');
342	
343	  // Pass credentials to Rust backend via Tauri IPC
344	  // The CipherBox JWT is sent as idToken -- backend verifies it with loginType 'corekit'
345	  await invoke('handle_auth_complete', {
346	    idToken: cipherboxJwt,
347	    privateKey: privKeyHex,
348	  });
349	  console.log('[CoreKit] Credentials passed to Rust backend');
350	
351	  // Clear MFA state
352	  temporaryAccessToken = null;
353	}
354	
355	/**
356	 * Restore session from a Core Kit init() that already has LOGGED_IN status.
357	 *
358	 * When Core Kit restores a previous session from localStorage during init(),
359	 * the TSS key is already available. This exports it and passes it to the
360	 * Rust backend via the session_restore IPC command (which skips /auth/login
361	 * since silent refresh already established API tokens).
362	 */
363	export async function restoreSession(): Promise<void> {
364	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
365	  if (coreKit.status !== COREKIT_STATUS.LOGGED_IN) {
366	    throw new Error(`Cannot restore session: Core Kit status is ${coreKit.status}`);
367	  }
368	
369	  console.log('[CoreKit] Restoring session from init...');
370	  const privateKeyHex = await coreKit._UNSAFE_exportTssKey();
371	  const privKeyHex = privateKeyHex.startsWith('0x') ? privateKeyHex.slice(2) : privateKeyHex;
372	  console.log('[CoreKit] TSS key exported for session restore');
373	
374	  await invoke('handle_session_restore', {
375	    privateKey: privKeyHex,
376	  });
377	  console.log('[CoreKit] Session restore complete');
378	}
379	
380	/**
381	 * Logout from Core Kit and clear Rust-side state.
382	 *
383	 * Calls the Rust logout command first (clears Keychain, zeros keys),
384	 * then disconnects from Core Kit.
385	 */
386	export async function logout(): Promise<void> {
387	  // Clear Rust-side state (Keychain, memory keys)
388	  await invoke('logout');
389	
390	  // Disconnect from Core Kit
391	  if (coreKit && coreKit.status === COREKIT_STATUS.LOGGED_IN) {
392	    try {
393	      await coreKit.logout();
394	    } catch (err) {
395	      // Best-effort -- don't fail logout if Core Kit cleanup fails
396	      console.warn('[CoreKit] Logout error (continuing):', err);
397	    }
398	  }
399	
400	  console.log('[CoreKit] Logout complete');
401	}
402	
403	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
404	// MFA Recovery and Device Approval
405	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
406	
407	/**
408	 * Get or create a persistent device ID for this desktop.
409	 * Stored in localStorage keyed by 'cipherbox-desktop-device-id'.
410	 * Format: SHA-256 hex (64 chars) of a random 32-byte value.
411	 */
412	async function getDesktopDeviceId(): Promise<string> {
413	  const key = 'cipherbox-desktop-device-id';
414	  const stored = localStorage.getItem(key);
415	  if (stored && stored.length === 64) return stored;
416	
417	  // Generate a random 32-byte value and SHA-256 hash it
418	  const random = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32));
419	  const hash = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', random);
420	  const deviceId = bytesToHex(new Uint8Array(hash));
421	  localStorage.setItem(key, deviceId);
422	  return deviceId;
423	}
424	
425	/**
426	 * Get a human-readable device name for this desktop.
427	 * Uses navigator.userAgent to detect OS, prefixed with "CipherBox Desktop".
428	 */
429	function getDeviceName(): string {
430	  const ua = navigator.userAgent;
431	  if (/Macintosh|Mac OS X/i.test(ua)) return 'CipherBox Desktop on macOS';
432	  if (/Windows/i.test(ua)) return 'CipherBox Desktop on Windows';
433	  if (/Linux/i.test(ua)) return 'CipherBox Desktop on Linux';
434	  return 'CipherBox Desktop';
435	}
436	
437	/**
438	 * Get the access token for API calls.
439	 * Uses temporary token (REQUIRED_SHARE state) or null.
440	 */
441	function getAccessToken(): string | null {
442	  return temporaryAccessToken;
443	}
444	
445	/**
446	 * Recover from REQUIRED_SHARE using a 24-word recovery mnemonic.
447	 *
448	 * Flow:
449	 * 1. Convert mnemonic to factor key hex via mnemonicToKey
450	 * 2. Input factor key to Core Kit (transitions from REQUIRED_SHARE to LOGGED_IN)
451	 * 3. Create a device factor for this desktop so future logins skip MFA
452	 * 4. Export TSS key and complete auth (pass to Rust backend)
453	 *
454	 * @param mnemonic - 24-word BIP39 recovery phrase
455	 */
456	export async function inputRecoveryPhrase(mnemonic: string): Promise<void> {
457	  if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
458	  if (coreKit.status !== COREKIT_STATUS.REQUIRED_SHARE) {
459	    throw new Error('Not in REQUIRED_SHARE state');
460	  }
461	
462	  // Convert mnemonic to factor key hex
463	  const factorKeyHex = mnemonicToKey(mnemonic.trim().toLowerCase());
464	
465	  // Input the factor key to Core Kit
466	  await coreKit.inputFactorKey(new BN(factorKeyHex, 'hex'));
467	
468	  // After successful input, Core Kit status should be LOGGED_IN
469	  // Cast needed: TS narrows status to REQUIRED_SHARE from earlier guard,
470	  // but inputFactorKey mutates the status to LOGGED_IN
471	  if ((coreKit.status as string) !== COREKIT_STATUS.LOGGED_IN) {
472	    throw new Error('Recovery failed: invalid recovery phrase');
473	  }
474	  console.log('[MFA] Recovery phrase accepted, status:', coreKit.status);
475	
476	  // Create a device factor for this desktop so future logins don't need recovery
477	  const deviceId = await getDesktopDeviceId();
478	  const newDeviceFactor = generateFactorKey();
479	  await coreKit.createFactor({
480	    shareType: TssShareType.DEVICE,
481	    factorKey: newDeviceFactor.private,
482	    shareDescription: FactorKeyTypeShareDescription.DeviceShare,
483	    additionalMetadata: {
484	      deviceId,
485	      platform: 'macos',
486	      name: getDeviceName(),
487	    },
488	  });
489	  await coreKit.setDeviceFactor(newDeviceFactor.private);
490	  await coreKit.commitChanges();
491	  console.log('[MFA] Device factor created for future logins');
492	
493	  // Export key and complete auth
494	  if (!lastCipherboxJwt) throw new Error('No stored JWT for auth completion');
495	  await completeAuthHandoff(lastCipherboxJwt);
496	}
497	
498	/**
499	 * Create a device approval request on the CipherBox bulletin board.
500	 *
501	 * Generates an ephemeral secp256k1 keypair for ECIES key exchange.
502	 * The private key is stored in module scope and used to decrypt the
503	 * factor key when the request is approved.
504	 *
505	 * @returns requestId for polling, and stores ephemeral private key in module scope
506	 */
507	let ephemeralPrivateKey: Uint8Array | null = null;
508	
509	export async function requestDeviceApproval(): Promise<string> {
510	  const token = getAccessToken();
511	  if (!token) throw new Error('No access token available');
512	
513	  // Generate ephemeral secp256k1 keypair for ECIES key exchange
514	  const ephemeral = secp256k1.keygen();
515	  ephemeralPrivateKey = ephemeral.secretKey;
516	  const ephemeralPubKeyHex = bytesToHex(ephemeral.publicKey);
517	
518	  const deviceId = await getDesktopDeviceId();
519	
520	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/device-approval/request`, {
521	    method: 'POST',
522	    headers: {
523	      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
524	      Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,
525	    },
526	    body: JSON.stringify({
527	      deviceId,
528	      deviceName: getDeviceName(),
529	      ephemeralPublicKey: ephemeralPubKeyHex,
530	    }),
531	  });
532	  if (!resp.ok) throw new Error('Failed to create approval request');
533	  const { requestId } = await resp.json();
534	  console.log('[MFA] Device approval request created:', requestId);
535	  return requestId;
536	}
537	
538	/**
539	 * Poll the status of a device approval request.
540	 *
541	 * When approved, ECIES-decrypts the factor key with the ephemeral private key,
542	 * inputs it to Core Kit, creates a device factor, and completes auth.
543	 *
544	 * @returns Current status of the approval request
545	 */
546	export async function pollApprovalStatus(
547	  requestId: string
548	): Promise<'pending' | 'approved' | 'denied' | 'expired'> {
549	  const token = getAccessToken();
550	  if (!token) throw new Error('No access token available');
551	
552	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/device-approval/${requestId}/status`, {
553	    headers: {
554	      Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,
555	    },
556	  });
557	  if (!resp.ok) throw new Error('Failed to check approval status');
558	  const { status, encryptedFactorKey } = await resp.json();
559	
560	  if (status === 'approved' && encryptedFactorKey) {
561	    if (!ephemeralPrivateKey) throw new Error('Ephemeral private key not available');
562	    if (!coreKit) throw new Error('Core Kit not initialized');
563	
564	    // ECIES-decrypt the factor key using the ephemeral private key
565	    const encrypted = hexToBytes(encryptedFactorKey);
566	    const factorKeyBytes = await unwrapKey(encrypted, ephemeralPrivateKey);
567	    const factorKeyHex = bytesToHex(factorKeyBytes);
568	    factorKeyBytes.fill(0); // Zero-fill after conversion
569	
570	    // Clear ephemeral key from memory
571	    ephemeralPrivateKey.fill(0);
572	    ephemeralPrivateKey = null;
573	
574	    // Input the factor key to complete Core Kit login
575	    await coreKit.inputFactorKey(new BN(factorKeyHex, 'hex'));
576	    console.log('[MFA] Factor key accepted, status:', coreKit.status);
577	
578	    // Create device factor for future logins
579	    const deviceId = await getDesktopDeviceId();
580	    const newDeviceFactor = generateFactorKey();
581	    await coreKit.createFactor({
582	      shareType: TssShareType.DEVICE,
583	      factorKey: newDeviceFactor.private,
584	      shareDescription: FactorKeyTypeShareDescription.DeviceShare,
585	      additionalMetadata: {
586	        deviceId,
587	        platform: 'macos',
588	        name: getDeviceName(),
589	      },
590	    });
591	    await coreKit.setDeviceFactor(newDeviceFactor.private);
592	    await coreKit.commitChanges();
593	    console.log('[MFA] Device factor created after approval');
594	
595	    // Export key and complete auth
596	    if (!lastCipherboxJwt) throw new Error('No stored JWT for auth completion');
597	    await completeAuthHandoff(lastCipherboxJwt);
598	
599	    return 'approved';
600	  }
601	
602	  return status;
603	}
604	
605	/**
606	 * Cancel a pending device approval request.
607	 * Cleans up the ephemeral private key from memory.
608	 */
609	export async function cancelApprovalRequest(requestId: string): Promise<void> {
610	  const token = getAccessToken();
611	  if (!token) return;
612	
613	  try {
614	    await fetch(`${API_BASE}/device-approval/${requestId}`, {
615	      method: 'DELETE',
616	      headers: {
617	        Authorization: `Bearer ${token}`,
618	      },
619	    });
620	  } catch {
621	    // Best-effort cleanup -- request may already be expired
622	  }
623	
624	  // Clear ephemeral key
625	  if (ephemeralPrivateKey) {
626	    ephemeralPrivateKey.fill(0);
627	    ephemeralPrivateKey = null;
628	  }
629	}
630	
631	/**
632	 * Approve a device approval request (API-complete stub).
633	 *
634	 * This function is fully implemented and tested, but has no UI trigger
635	 * in Phase 11.1. Users must approve devices from the web app.
636	 *
637	 * Phase 11.2 will add approval notification UI (tray icon notification +
638	 * approval dialog when desktop is the approving device).
639	 */
640	export async function approveDevice(
641	  requestId: string,
642	  ephemeralPublicKeyHex: string
643	): Promise<void> {
644	  if (!coreKit || coreKit.status !== COREKIT_STATUS.LOGGED_IN) {
645	    throw new Error('Must be logged in to approve devices');
646	  }
647	
648	  // Get current factor key from Core Kit
649	  const factorKeyResult = coreKit.getCurrentFactorKey();
650	  if (!factorKeyResult?.factorKey) {
651	    throw new Error('No active factor key available to share');
652	  }
653	  const factorKeyHex = factorKeyResult.factorKey.toString('hex').padStart(64, '0');
654	  const factorKeyBytes = hexToBytes(factorKeyHex);
655	
656	  // ECIES-encrypt the factor key with the requester's ephemeral public key
657	  const ephemeralPubKey = hexToBytes(ephemeralPublicKeyHex);
658	  const encrypted = await wrapKey(factorKeyBytes, ephemeralPubKey);
659	  factorKeyBytes.fill(0); // Zero-fill after wrapping
660	
661	  // Get device ID for tracking
662	  const deviceId = await getDesktopDeviceId();
663	
664	  // Send approval response to the bulletin board
665	  const resp = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/device-approval/${requestId}/respond`, {
666	    method: 'POST',
667	    headers: {
668	      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
669	      Authorization: `Bearer ${temporaryAccessToken || ''}`,
670	    },
671	    body: JSON.stringify({
672	      action: 'approve',
673	      encryptedFactorKey: bytesToHex(encrypted),
674	      respondedByDeviceId: deviceId,
675	    }),
676	  });
677	  if (!resp.ok) throw new Error('Failed to approve device');
678	  console.log('[MFA] Device approved:', requestId);
679	}
680	
681	// TODO (Phase 11.2): Add approval listener after auth success to poll for pending
682	// approval requests and show native notification when approval is needed.
683	
684	/**
685	 * Get a Google credential (ID token) via OAuth2 implicit flow in a popup.
686	 *
687	 * GIS One Tap doesn't work in Tauri webview (no Google session, unregistered
688	 * origin). Instead, open a proper Google OAuth consent page in a popup window.
689	 *
690	 * **Redirect URI handling:**
691	 * Google OAuth rejects custom URI schemes like `tauri://localhost` (used in
692	 * production Tauri builds on macOS). To solve this, the Rust backend starts
693	 * a temporary localhost HTTP server that receives the OAuth redirect. The
694	 * callback page extracts the token from the URL fragment and POSTs it back
695	 * to the server, which emits a Tauri event to the main webview.
696	 *
697	 * In dev mode (`http://localhost:1420` origin), the localhost callback server
698	 * is still used for consistency.
699	 *
700	 * Requires `http://localhost` (with the callback server port) to be registered
701	 * as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console. Since the port is
702	 * dynamic, register `http://localhost` as an authorized JavaScript origin and
703	 * add a few specific redirect URIs or use a loopback redirect approach.
704	 *
705	 * **Google's loopback IP redirect policy:**
706	 * For desktop apps, Google allows `http://localhost:<port>/path` redirect URIs
707	 * without pre-registering each port, as long as `http://localhost` is
708	 * registered as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console.
709	 */
710	function getGoogleCredential(): Promise<string> {
711	  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
712	    if (!GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID) {
713	      reject(new Error('Google Client ID not configured (VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID)'));
714	      return;
715	    }
716	
717	    let unlisten: UnlistenFn | null = null;
718	    let timeout: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
719	
720	    const cleanup = () => {
721	      if (unlisten) {
722	        unlisten();
723	        unlisten = null;
724	      }
725	      if (timeout) {
726	        clearTimeout(timeout);
727	        timeout = null;
728	      }
729	    };
730	
731	    const nonce = crypto.randomUUID();
732	    const state = crypto.randomUUID();
733	
734	    // Start the OAuth callback server and open the popup
735	    (async () => {
736	      try {
737	        // Start temporary localhost HTTP server for OAuth callback
738	        const port: number = await invoke('start_oauth_server');
739	        const redirectUri = `http://localhost:${port}/callback`;
740	        console.log('[Google Auth] OAuth callback server on port', port);
741	
742	        // Listen for the oauth-callback Tauri event from the Rust server
743	        unlisten = await listen<{
744	          id_token: string | null;
745	          error: string | null;
746	          state: string | null;
747	        }>('oauth-callback', (event) => {
748	          cleanup();
749	          console.log('[Google Auth] Received oauth-callback event');
750	
751	          const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;
752	
753	          if (error) {
754	            reject(new Error(error));
755	            return;
756	          }
757	
758	          // CSRF validation: verify state matches
759	          if (returnedState !== state) {
760	            reject(new Error('OAuth state mismatch (possible CSRF)'));
761	            return;
762	          }
763	
764	          if (id_token) {
765	            resolve(id_token);
766	          } else {
767	            reject(new Error('No ID token received from Google'));
768	          }
769	        });
770	
771	        const params = new URLSearchParams({
772	          client_id: GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID,
773	          redirect_uri: redirectUri,
774	          response_type: 'id_token',
775	          scope: 'openid email profile',
776	          nonce,
777	          state,
778	          prompt: 'select_account',
779	        });
780	
781	        const authUrl = `https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth?${params}`;
782	
783	        console.log('[Google Auth] Opening OAuth popup...');
784	        await invoke('open_oauth_popup', { url: authUrl });
785	
786	        // Timeout after 2 minutes
787	        timeout = setTimeout(() => {
788	          cleanup();
789	          console.error('[Google Auth] Timed out after 2 minutes');
790	          reject(new Error('Google authentication timed out'));
791	        }, 120000);
792	      } catch (err) {
793	        cleanup();
794	        console.error('[Google Auth] Failed to start OAuth flow:', err);
795	        reject(new Error(`Failed to start OAuth flow: ${err}`));
796	      }
797	    })();
798	  });
799	}
800	
```

> AGENT

The changes look solid. Here's the summary:

## Root Cause

`getGoogleCredential()` was building the redirect URI from `window.location.origin`, which resolves to `tauri://localhost` in production Tauri builds. Google OAuth only accepts `http://` and `https://` redirect URIs.

## Fix Applied (3 files)

1. **`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/oauth.rs`** — New `start_oauth_server` command that spins up a temporary `127.0.0.1` HTTP server. Tries preferred ports 14200/14201/14202 (pre-registerable in Google Console), falls back to random. Serves a callback page that extracts the token from the URL fragment and POSTs it back, then emits a Tauri `oauth-callback` event. Auto-terminates after 120s.

2. **`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs`** — Registered `start_oauth_server` in both debug and release invoke handlers.

3. **`apps/desktop/src/auth.ts`** — `getGoogleCredential()` now calls `start_oauth_server` first, uses `http://localhost:{port}/callback` as redirect URI, and listens for the `oauth-callback` Tauri event instead of polling localStorage. CSRF state validation preserved.

## Before You Can Test

Add these authorized redirect URIs in **Google Cloud Console** for client `164556913424-uif44is1apnl7rqc9a2ac7vn8u9dq0ee`:

- `http://localhost:14200/callback`
- `http://localhost:14201/callback`
- `http://localhost:14202/callback`

Then run `pnpm --filter desktop dev` and click the Google login button.

Does this look right? Want to test it, or should I adjust anything?

> AGENT

The problem is the single `read()` call on line 216 — headers and POST body can arrive in separate TCP segments. The server reads the headers, sees `POST /token`, but the body hasn't arrived yet, so JSON parsing fails.

I need to add Content-Length-aware body reading:

> TOOL

```
-//! OAuth popup window command.
+//! OAuth popup window and callback server commands.
 
 use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
+use tauri::{Emitter, Manager};
+use tokio::io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncWriteExt};
+use tokio::net::TcpListener;
 
 /// Counter for unique OAuth popup window labels (shared with tray handler).
 static POPUP_COUNTER: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
@@ -45,3 +48,299 @@ pub async fn open_oauth_popup(app: tauri::AppHandle, url: String) -> Result<(),
 
     Ok(())
 }
+
+/// Preferred ports for the OAuth callback server, tried in order.
+///
+/// These must be registered as authorized redirect URIs in Google Cloud Console:
+///   - `http://localhost:14200/callback`
+///   - `http://localhost:14201/callback`
+///   - `http://localhost:14202/callback`
+///
+/// If none of the preferred ports are available, falls back to a random port.
+/// A random port requires `http://localhost` (without port) to be registered
+/// in Google Cloud Console, which Google allows for Desktop-type OAuth clients.
+const PREFERRED_PORTS: &[u16] = &[14200, 14201, 14202];
+
+/// Start a temporary localhost HTTP server to receive OAuth callbacks.
+///
+/// Google OAuth rejects non-HTTP(S) redirect URIs like `tauri://localhost`.
+/// In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to
+/// `tauri://localhost` (macOS) or `https://tauri.localhost` (Windows),
+/// which Google won't accept.
+///
+/// This command spins up a short-lived HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`,
+/// trying preferred ports first (so the redirect URI can be pre-registered
+/// in Google Cloud Console). The server:
+///   1. Serves a callback HTML page at any GET request that extracts
+///      the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back
+///   2. Receives the POST at `/token`, emits a Tauri event, and shuts down
+///
+/// Returns the port number so the frontend can build the redirect_uri:
+///   `http://localhost:{port}/callback`
+///
+/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds if no callback arrives.
+#[tauri::command]
+pub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<u16, String> {
+    // Try preferred ports first (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)
+    let mut listener = None;
+    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {
+        match TcpListener::bind(format!("127.0.0.1:{}", port)).await {
+            Ok(l) => {
+                log::info!("OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}", port);
+                listener = Some(l);
+                break;
+            }
+            Err(e) => {
+                log::debug!("Preferred port {} unavailable: {}", port, e);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    // Fallback: random port
+    let listener = match listener {
+        Some(l) => l,
+        None => {
+            log::warn!(
+                "All preferred ports {:?} unavailable, using random port",
+                PREFERRED_PORTS
+            );
+            TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
+                .await
+                .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to bind OAuth callback server: {}", e))?
+        }
+    };
+
+    let port = listener
+        .local_addr()
+        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to get server address: {}", e))?
+        .port();
+
+    log::info!("OAuth callback server started on port {}", port);
+
+    // Spawn the server task — it will shut down after receiving the callback
+    // or after the timeout (120s).
+    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app));
+
+    Ok(port)
+}
+
+/// Minimal callback HTML page served by the OAuth callback server.
+///
+/// This page runs in the OAuth popup webview after Google redirects back.
+/// It extracts the token from the URL fragment (implicit flow uses fragments)
+/// and POSTs it back to the same localhost server, which then emits a Tauri event.
+const CALLBACK_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
+<html>
+<head>
+<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
+</head>
+<body>
+<p id="msg">Completing sign-in...</p>
+<script>
+(function() {
+  var hash = window.location.hash.substring(1);
+  var params = new URLSearchParams(hash);
+  var idToken = params.get('id_token');
+  var error = params.get('error');
+  var state = params.get('state');
+
+  // POST the extracted fragment data back to the same server
+  fetch('/token', {
+    method: 'POST',
+    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
+    body: JSON.stringify({
+      id_token: idToken || null,
+      error: error || null,
+      state: state || null
+    })
+  })
+  .then(function() {
+    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Sign-in complete. You can close this window.';
+  })
+  .catch(function(err) {
+    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;
+  });
+})();
+</script>
+</body>
+</html>"#;
+
+/// Response page shown after the token POST is received.
+const DONE_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
+<html>
+<head>
+<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
+</head>
+<body>
+<p>Sign-in complete. You can close this window.</p>
+</body>
+</html>"#;
+
+/// OAuth callback data emitted via Tauri event.
+#[derive(Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
+struct OAuthCallbackPayload {
+    id_token: Option<String>,
+    error: Option<String>,
+    state: Option<String>,
+}
+
+/// Run the OAuth callback server until a token is received or timeout.
+async fn run_oauth_server(listener: TcpListener, app: tauri::AppHandle) {
+    let timeout = tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);
+
+    loop {
+        let accept_result = tokio::select! {
+            result = listener.accept() => result,
+            _ = tokio::time::sleep(timeout) => {
+                log::warn!("OAuth callback server timed out after 120s");
+                // Emit error event so the frontend doesn't hang
+                let _ = app.emit("oauth-callback", OAuthCallbackPayload {
+                    id_token: None,
+                    error: Some("OAuth callback server timed out".to_string()),
+                    state: None,
+                });
+                return;
+            }
+        };
+
+        let (mut stream, _addr) = match accept_result {
+            Ok(conn) => conn,
+            Err(e) => {
+                log::error!("OAuth server accept error: {}", e);
+                continue;
+            }
+        };
+
+        // Read the HTTP request — headers may arrive before the body,
+        // so we may need multiple reads for POST requests.
+        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];
+        let mut total = 0usize;
+        // Initial read
+        match stream.read(&mut buf).await {
+            Ok(n) if n > 0 => total = n,
+            _ => continue,
+        };
+
+        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
+
+        // Parse the first line to determine method and path
+        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
+
+        if first_line.starts_with("POST /token") {
+            // Parse Content-Length from headers to know how much body to expect
+            let content_length = initial_request
+                .lines()
+                .find_map(|line| {
+                    let lower = line.to_ascii_lowercase();
+                    if lower.starts_with("content-length:") {
+                        lower.trim_start_matches("content-length:").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()
+                    } else {
+                        None
+                    }
+                })
+                .unwrap_or(0);
+
+            // Find where headers end (\r\n\r\n) and calculate how much body we have
+            let header_end = initial_request.find("\r\n\r\n").map(|i| i + 4);
+            if let Some(header_len) = header_end {
+                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);
+                let remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);
+                // Read remaining body bytes if we don't have everything yet
+                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= buf.len() {
+                    let mut left = remaining;
+                    while left > 0 {
+                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {
+                            Ok(0) => break,
+                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }
+                            Err(_) => break,
+                        }
+                    }
+                }
+            }
+
+            // Re-parse with full data
+            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
+            let body = full_request
+                .split("\r\n\r\n")
+                .nth(1)
+                .unwrap_or("");
+
+            let payload: OAuthCallbackPayload =
+                serde_json::from_str(body).unwrap_or(OAuthCallbackPayload {
+                    id_token: None,
+                    error: Some("Failed to parse callback data".to_string()),
+                    state: None,
+                });
+
+            let has_token = payload.id_token.is_some();
+            log::info!(
+                "OAuth callback received: has_token={}, error={:?}",
+                has_token,
+                payload.error
+            );
+
+            // Emit the Tauri event to the main webview
+            if let Err(e) = app.emit("oauth-callback", payload) {
+                log::error!("Failed to emit oauth-callback event: {}", e);
+            }
+
+            // Send response and close
+            let response = format!(
+                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
+                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
+                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
+                 Connection: close\r\n\
+                 Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
+                 \r\n\
+                 {}",
+                DONE_HTML.len(),
+                DONE_HTML
+            );
+            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
+            let _ = stream.flush().await;
+
+            // Also close any remaining oauth-popup windows
+            close_oauth_popups(&app);
+
+            log::info!("OAuth callback server shutting down");
+            return;
+        } else if first_line.starts_with("OPTIONS") {
+            // Handle CORS preflight for the POST
+            let response = "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
+                            Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
+                            Access-Control-Allow-Methods: POST\r\n\
+                            Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type\r\n\
+                            Content-Length: 0\r\n\
+                            Connection: close\r\n\
+                            \r\n";
+            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
+            let _ = stream.flush().await;
+        } else {
+            // Any other GET request (the callback page)
+            let response = format!(
+                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
+                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
+                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
+                 Connection: close\r\n\
+                 \r\n\
+                 {}",
+                CALLBACK_HTML.len(),
+                CALLBACK_HTML
+            );
+            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
+            let _ = stream.flush().await;
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// Close all oauth-popup windows.
+fn close_oauth_popups(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {
+    // Popup labels are "oauth-popup-0", "oauth-popup-1", etc.
+    // Try to close a reasonable range.
+    for i in 0..100 {
+        let label = format!("oauth-popup-{}", i);
+        if let Some(window) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {
+            let _ = window.close();
+        }
+    }
+}

+ * to the server, which emits a Tauri event to the main webview.

+ * In dev mode (`http://localhost:1420` origin), the localhost callback server
+ * is still used for consistency.
+ *

+ * as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console. Since the port is
+ * dynamic, register `http://localhost` as an authorized JavaScript origin and
+ * add a few specific redirect URIs or use a loopback redirect approach.
+ *
+ * **Google's loopback IP redirect policy:**
+ * For desktop apps, Google allows `http://localhost:<port>/path` redirect URIs
+ * without pre-registering each port, as long as `http://localhost` is
+ * registered as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console.
  */
```

The branch is pushed. Suggested PR details:

**Title:** `fix(desktop): use localhost callback server for Google OAuth`

**Body:**
- Google OAuth rejected `tauri://localhost` redirect URI in production Tauri builds (Error 400: invalid_request)
- Added a temporary localhost HTTP server (`start_oauth_server` Tauri command) that receives the OAuth callback, extracts the id_token from the URL fragment, and emits it back via a Tauri event
- Rewrote `getGoogleCredential()` to use the callback server instead of localStorage polling
- Added `core:event:default` Tauri capability for event API access

Don't forget to add the redirect URIs (`http://localhost:14200/callback`, `14201`, `14202`) to the Google Cloud Console before testing in production builds.

```bash
  --body "## Summary

- Google OAuth rejected \`tauri://localhost\` redirect URI in production Tauri builds (Error 400: invalid_request)
- Added a temporary localhost HTTP server (\`start_oauth_server\` Tauri command) that receives the OAuth callback on \`http://localhost:{port}/callback\`, extracts the id_token from the URL fragment, and emits it back via a Tauri event
- Rewrote \`getGoogleCredential()\` to use the callback server + Tauri event listener instead of the previous localStorage-polling approach
- Added \`core:event:default\` Tauri capability for event API access

## Setup required

Add these authorized redirect URIs to the Google OAuth client in Google Cloud Console:

- \`http://localhost:14200/callback\`
- \`http://localhost:14201/callback\`
- \`http://localhost:14202/callback\`

- [ ] Run \`pnpm --filter desktop dev\`, click Google login — OAuth popup opens, login completes
- [ ] Verify email OTP login still works (unaffected code path)
- [ ] Build a release build and verify Google login works with \`tauri://localhost\` origin"
```

```
1	//! OAuth popup window and callback server commands.
2	
3	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
4	use tauri::{Emitter, Manager};
5	use tokio::io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncWriteExt};
6	use tokio::net::TcpListener;
7	
8	/// Counter for unique OAuth popup window labels (shared with tray handler).
9	static POPUP_COUNTER: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
10	
11	/// Open an OAuth popup window directly from Rust.
12	///
13	/// Bypasses `window.open()` which is unreliable on Windows WebView2 (the
14	/// `NewWindowRequested` event / `on_new_window` handler may silently fail).
15	/// Instead, the webview calls this command via `invoke()` to create a new
16	/// Tauri webview window pointing directly at the OAuth URL.
17	#[tauri::command]
18	pub async fn open_oauth_popup(app: tauri::AppHandle, url: String) -> Result<(), String> {
19	    let n = POPUP_COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
20	    let label = format!("oauth-popup-{}", n);
21	
22	    let parsed_url: tauri::Url = url
23	        .parse()
24	        .map_err(|e| format!("Invalid OAuth URL: {}", e))?;
25	
26	    // Allowlist: only HTTPS requests to known OAuth providers are permitted.
27	    const ALLOWED_HOSTS: &[&str] = &["accounts.google.com"];
28	    if parsed_url.scheme() != "https" {
29	        return Err("OAuth URL must use HTTPS".to_string());
30	    }
31	    let host = parsed_url.host_str().unwrap_or("");
32	    if !ALLOWED_HOSTS.contains(&host) {
33	        return Err(format!("OAuth URL host '{}' is not allowed", host));
34	    }
35	
36	    log::info!("Creating OAuth popup window: {} -> {}", label, host);
37	
38	    tauri::WebviewWindowBuilder::new(
39	        &app,
40	        &label,
41	        tauri::WebviewUrl::External(parsed_url),
42	    )
43	    .title("Sign in with Google")
44	    .inner_size(500.0, 700.0)
45	    .center()
46	    .build()
47	    .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create OAuth popup: {}", e))?;
48	
49	    Ok(())
50	}
51	
52	/// Preferred ports for the OAuth callback server, tried in order.
53	///
54	/// These must be registered as authorized redirect URIs in Google Cloud Console:
55	///   - `http://localhost:14200/callback`
56	///   - `http://localhost:14201/callback`
57	///   - `http://localhost:14202/callback`
58	///
59	/// If none of the preferred ports are available, falls back to a random port.
60	/// A random port requires `http://localhost` (without port) to be registered
61	/// in Google Cloud Console, which Google allows for Desktop-type OAuth clients.
62	const PREFERRED_PORTS: &[u16] = &[14200, 14201, 14202];
63	
64	/// Start a temporary localhost HTTP server to receive OAuth callbacks.
65	///
66	/// Google OAuth rejects non-HTTP(S) redirect URIs like `tauri://localhost`.
67	/// In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to
68	/// `tauri://localhost` (macOS) or `https://tauri.localhost` (Windows),
69	/// which Google won't accept.
70	///
71	/// This command spins up a short-lived HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`,
72	/// trying preferred ports first (so the redirect URI can be pre-registered
73	/// in Google Cloud Console). The server:
74	///   1. Serves a callback HTML page at any GET request that extracts
75	///      the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back
76	///   2. Receives the POST at `/token`, emits a Tauri event, and shuts down
77	///
78	/// Returns the port number so the frontend can build the redirect_uri:
79	///   `http://localhost:{port}/callback`
80	///
81	/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds if no callback arrives.
82	#[tauri::command]
83	pub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<u16, String> {
84	    // Try preferred ports first (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)
85	    let mut listener = None;
86	    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {
87	        match TcpListener::bind(format!("127.0.0.1:{}", port)).await {
88	            Ok(l) => {
89	                log::info!("OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}", port);
90	                listener = Some(l);
91	                break;
92	            }
93	            Err(e) => {
94	                log::debug!("Preferred port {} unavailable: {}", port, e);
95	            }
96	        }
97	    }
98	
99	    // Fallback: random port
100	    let listener = match listener {
101	        Some(l) => l,
102	        None => {
103	            log::warn!(
104	                "All preferred ports {:?} unavailable, using random port",
105	                PREFERRED_PORTS
106	            );
107	            TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0")
108	                .await
109	                .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to bind OAuth callback server: {}", e))?
110	        }
111	    };
112	
113	    let port = listener
114	        .local_addr()
115	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to get server address: {}", e))?
116	        .port();
117	
118	    log::info!("OAuth callback server started on port {}", port);
119	
120	    // Spawn the server task — it will shut down after receiving the callback
121	    // or after the timeout (120s).
122	    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app));
123	
124	    Ok(port)
125	}
126	
127	/// Minimal callback HTML page served by the OAuth callback server.
128	///
129	/// This page runs in the OAuth popup webview after Google redirects back.
130	/// It extracts the token from the URL fragment (implicit flow uses fragments)
131	/// and POSTs it back to the same localhost server, which then emits a Tauri event.
132	const CALLBACK_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
133	<html>
134	<head>
135	<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
136	</head>
137	<body>
138	<p id="msg">Completing sign-in...</p>
139	<script>
140	(function() {
141	  var hash = window.location.hash.substring(1);
142	  var params = new URLSearchParams(hash);
143	  var idToken = params.get('id_token');
144	  var error = params.get('error');
145	  var state = params.get('state');
146	
147	  // POST the extracted fragment data back to the same server
148	  fetch('/token', {
149	    method: 'POST',
150	    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
151	    body: JSON.stringify({
152	      id_token: idToken || null,
153	      error: error || null,
154	      state: state || null
155	    })
156	  })
157	  .then(function() {
158	    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Sign-in complete. You can close this window.';
159	  })
160	  .catch(function(err) {
161	    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;
162	  });
163	})();
164	</script>
165	</body>
166	</html>"#;
167	
168	/// Response page shown after the token POST is received.
169	const DONE_HTML: &str = r#"<!doctype html>
170	<html>
171	<head>
172	<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>
173	</head>
174	<body>
175	<p>Sign-in complete. You can close this window.</p>
176	</body>
177	</html>"#;
178	
179	/// OAuth callback data emitted via Tauri event.
180	#[derive(Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
181	struct OAuthCallbackPayload {
182	    id_token: Option<String>,
183	    error: Option<String>,
184	    state: Option<String>,
185	}
186	
187	/// Run the OAuth callback server until a token is received or timeout.
188	async fn run_oauth_server(listener: TcpListener, app: tauri::AppHandle) {
189	    let timeout = tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);
190	
191	    loop {
192	        let accept_result = tokio::select! {
193	            result = listener.accept() => result,
194	            _ = tokio::time::sleep(timeout) => {
195	                log::warn!("OAuth callback server timed out after 120s");
196	                // Emit error event so the frontend doesn't hang
197	                let _ = app.emit("oauth-callback", OAuthCallbackPayload {
198	                    id_token: None,
199	                    error: Some("OAuth callback server timed out".to_string()),
200	                    state: None,
201	                });
202	                return;
203	            }
204	        };
205	
206	        let (mut stream, _addr) = match accept_result {
207	            Ok(conn) => conn,
208	            Err(e) => {
209	                log::error!("OAuth server accept error: {}", e);
210	                continue;
211	            }
212	        };
213	
214	        // Read the HTTP request — headers may arrive before the body,
215	        // so we may need multiple reads for POST requests.
216	        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];
217	        let mut total = 0usize;
218	        // Initial read
219	        match stream.read(&mut buf).await {
220	            Ok(n) if n > 0 => total = n,
221	            _ => continue,
222	        };
223	
224	        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
225	
226	        // Parse the first line to determine method and path
227	        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
228	
229	        if first_line.starts_with("POST /token") {
230	            // Parse Content-Length from headers to know how much body to expect
231	            let content_length = initial_request
232	                .lines()
233	                .find_map(|line| {
234	                    let lower = line.to_ascii_lowercase();
235	                    if lower.starts_with("content-length:") {
236	                        lower.trim_start_matches("content-length:").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()
237	                    } else {
238	                        None
239	                    }
240	                })
241	                .unwrap_or(0);
242	
243	            // Find where headers end (\r\n\r\n) and calculate how much body we have
244	            let header_end = initial_request.find("\r\n\r\n").map(|i| i + 4);
245	            if let Some(header_len) = header_end {
246	                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);
247	                let remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);
248	                // Read remaining body bytes if we don't have everything yet
249	                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= buf.len() {
250	                    let mut left = remaining;
251	                    while left > 0 {
252	                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {
253	                            Ok(0) => break,
254	                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }
255	                            Err(_) => break,
256	                        }
257	                    }
258	                }
259	            }
260	
261	            // Re-parse with full data
262	            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
263	            let body = full_request
264	                .split("\r\n\r\n")
265	                .nth(1)
266	                .unwrap_or("");
267	
268	            let payload: OAuthCallbackPayload =
269	                serde_json::from_str(body).unwrap_or(OAuthCallbackPayload {
270	                    id_token: None,
271	                    error: Some("Failed to parse callback data".to_string()),
272	                    state: None,
273	                });
274	
275	            let has_token = payload.id_token.is_some();
276	            log::info!(
277	                "OAuth callback received: has_token={}, error={:?}",
278	                has_token,
279	                payload.error
280	            );
281	
282	            // Emit the Tauri event to the main webview
283	            if let Err(e) = app.emit("oauth-callback", payload) {
284	                log::error!("Failed to emit oauth-callback event: {}", e);
285	            }
286	
287	            // Send response and close
288	            let response = format!(
289	                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
290	                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
291	                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
292	                 Connection: close\r\n\
293	                 Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
294	                 \r\n\
295	                 {}",
296	                DONE_HTML.len(),
297	                DONE_HTML
298	            );
299	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
300	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
301	
302	            // Also close any remaining oauth-popup windows
303	            close_oauth_popups(&app);
304	
305	            log::info!("OAuth callback server shutting down");
306	            return;
307	        } else if first_line.starts_with("OPTIONS") {
308	            // Handle CORS preflight for the POST
309	            let response = "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
310	                            Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *\r\n\
311	                            Access-Control-Allow-Methods: POST\r\n\
312	                            Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type\r\n\
313	                            Content-Length: 0\r\n\
314	                            Connection: close\r\n\
315	                            \r\n";
316	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
317	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
318	        } else {
319	            // Any other GET request (the callback page)
320	            let response = format!(
321	                "HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n\
322	                 Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
323	                 Content-Length: {}\r\n\
324	                 Connection: close\r\n\
325	                 \r\n\
326	                 {}",
327	                CALLBACK_HTML.len(),
328	                CALLBACK_HTML
329	            );
330	            let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await;
331	            let _ = stream.flush().await;
332	        }
333	    }
334	}
335	
336	/// Close all oauth-popup windows.
337	fn close_oauth_popups(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {
338	    // Popup labels are "oauth-popup-0", "oauth-popup-1", etc.
339	    // Try to close a reasonable range.
340	    for i in 0..100 {
341	        let label = format!("oauth-popup-{}", i);
342	        if let Some(window) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {
343	            let _ = window.close();
344	        }
345	    }
346	}
347
```
