685	 * Get a Google credential (ID token) via OAuth2 implicit flow in a popup.
686	 *
687	 * GIS One Tap doesn't work in Tauri webview (no Google session, unregistered
688	 * origin). Instead, open a proper Google OAuth consent page in a popup window.
689	 *
690	 * **Redirect URI handling:**
691	 * Google OAuth rejects custom URI schemes like `tauri://localhost` (used in
692	 * production Tauri builds on macOS). To solve this, the Rust backend starts
693	 * a temporary localhost HTTP server that receives the OAuth redirect. The
694	 * callback page extracts the token from the URL fragment and POSTs it back
695	 * to the server, which emits a Tauri event to the main webview.
696	 *
697	 * In dev mode (`http://localhost:1420` origin), the localhost callback server
698	 * is still used for consistency.
699	 *
700	 * Requires `http://localhost` (with the callback server port) to be registered
701	 * as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console. Since the port is
702	 * dynamic, register `http://localhost` as an authorized JavaScript origin and
703	 * add a few specific redirect URIs or use a loopback redirect approach.
704	 *
705	 * **Google's loopback IP redirect policy:**
706	 * For desktop apps, Google allows `http://localhost:<port>/path` redirect URIs
707	 * without pre-registering each port, as long as `http://localhost` is
708	 * registered as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console.
709	 */
710	function getGoogleCredential(): Promise<string> {
711	  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
712	    if (!GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID) {
713	      reject(new Error('Google Client ID not configured (VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID)'));
714	      return;
715	    }
716	
717	    let unlisten: UnlistenFn | null = null;
718	    let timeout: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
719	
720	    const cleanup = () => {
721	      if (unlisten) {
722	        unlisten();
723	        unlisten = null;
724	      }
725	      if (timeout) {
726	        clearTimeout(timeout);
727	        timeout = null;
728	      }
729	    };
730	
731	    const nonce = crypto.randomUUID();
732	    const state = crypto.randomUUID();
733	
734	    // Start the OAuth callback server and open the popup
735	    (async () => {
736	      try {
737	        // Start temporary localhost HTTP server for OAuth callback
738	        const port: number = await invoke('start_oauth_server');
739	        const redirectUri = `http://localhost:${port}/callback`;
740	        console.log('[Google Auth] OAuth callback server on port', port);
741	
742	        // Listen for the oauth-callback Tauri event from the Rust server
743	        unlisten = await listen<{
744	          id_token: string | null;
745	          error: string | null;
746	          state: string | null;
747	        }>('oauth-callback', (event) => {
748	          cleanup();
749	          console.log('[Google Auth] Received oauth-callback event');
750	
751	          const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;
752	
753	          if (error) {
754	            reject(new Error(error));
755	            return;
756	          }
757	
758	          // CSRF validation: verify state matches
759	          if (returnedState !== state) {
760	            reject(new Error('OAuth state mismatch (possible CSRF)'));
761	            return;
762	          }
763	
764	          if (id_token) {
765	            resolve(id_token);
766	          } else {
767	            reject(new Error('No ID token received from Google'));
768	          }
769	        });
770	
771	        const params = new URLSearchParams({
772	          client_id: GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID,
773	          redirect_uri: redirectUri,
774	          response_type: 'id_token',
775	          scope: 'openid email profile',
776	          nonce,
777	          state,
778	          prompt: 'select_account',
779	        });
780	
781	        const authUrl = `https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth?${params}`;
782	
783	        console.log('[Google Auth] Opening OAuth popup...');
784	        await invoke('open_oauth_popup', { url: authUrl });
785	
786	        // Timeout after 2 minutes
787	        timeout = setTimeout(() => {
788	          cleanup();
789	          console.error('[Google Auth] Timed out after 2 minutes');
790	          reject(new Error('Google authentication timed out'));
791	        }, 120000);
792	      } catch (err) {
793	        cleanup();
794	        console.error('[Google Auth] Failed to start OAuth flow:', err);
795	        reject(new Error(`Failed to start OAuth flow: ${err}`));
796	      }
797	    })();
798	  });
799	}
800

{

  "old_string": "/// Start a temporary localhost HTTP server to receive OAuth callbacks.\n///\n/// Google OAuth rejects non-HTTP(S) redirect URIs like `tauri://localhost`.\n/// In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to\n/// `tauri://localhost` (macOS) or `https://tauri.localhost` (Windows),\n/// which Google won't accept.\n///\n/// This command spins up a short-lived HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`,\n/// trying preferred ports first (so the redirect URI can be pre-registered\n/// in Google Cloud Console). The server:\n///   1. Serves a callback HTML page at any GET request that extracts\n///      the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back\n///   2. Receives the POST at `/token`, emits a Tauri event, and shuts down\n///\n/// Returns the port number so the frontend can build the redirect_uri:\n///   `http://localhost:{port}/callback`\n///\n/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds if no callback arrives.\n#[tauri::command]\npub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<u16, String> {\n    // Try preferred ports first (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)\n    let mut listener = None;\n    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {\n        match TcpListener::bind(format!(\"127.0.0.1:{}\", port)).await {\n            Ok(l) => {\n                log::info!(\"OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}\", port);\n                listener = Some(l);\n                break;\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                log::debug!(\"Preferred port {} unavailable: {}\", port, e);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Fallback: random port\n    let listener = match listener {\n        Some(l) => l,\n        None => {\n            log::warn!(\n                \"All preferred ports {:?} unavailable, using random port\",\n                PREFERRED_PORTS\n            );\n            TcpListener::bind(\"127.0.0.1:0\")\n                .await\n                .map_err(|e| format!(\"Failed to bind OAuth callback server: {}\", e))?\n        }\n    };\n\n    let port = listener\n        .local_addr()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"Failed to get server address: {}\", e))?\n        .port();\n\n    log::info!(\"OAuth callback server started on port {}\", port);\n\n    // Spawn the server task — it will shut down after receiving the callback\n    // or after the timeout (120s).\n    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app));\n\n    Ok(port)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Response from `start_oauth_server` — port and scoped event name.\n#[derive(Clone, serde::Serialize)]\npub struct OAuthServerInfo {\n    port: u16,\n    event_name: String,\n}\n\n/// Start a temporary localhost HTTP server to receive OAuth callbacks.\n///\n/// Google OAuth rejects non-HTTP(S) redirect URIs like `tauri://localhost`.\n/// In production Tauri builds, `window.location.origin` resolves to\n/// `tauri://localhost` (macOS) or `https://tauri.localhost` (Windows),\n/// which Google won't accept.\n///\n/// This command spins up a short-lived HTTP server on `127.0.0.1`,\n/// trying preferred ports in order (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console).\n/// Fails fast if none are available. The server:\n///   1. Serves a callback HTML page (with embedded nonce) that extracts\n///      the OAuth fragment (`#id_token=...`) and POSTs it back\n///   2. Receives the POST at `/token`, validates the nonce, emits a\n///      port-scoped Tauri event, and shuts down\n///\n/// Returns `{ port, event_name }` so the frontend can build the redirect_uri\n/// (`http://localhost:{port}/callback`) and listen for the correct event.\n///\n/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds (absolute deadline).\n#[tauri::command]\npub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<OAuthServerInfo, String> {\n    // Try preferred ports (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)\n    let mut listener = None;\n    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {\n        match TcpListener::bind(format!(\"127.0.0.1:{}\", port)).await {\n            Ok(l) => {\n                log::info!(\"OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}\", port);\n                listener = Some(l);\n                break;\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                log::debug!(\"Preferred port {} unavailable: {}\", port, e);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    let listener = listener.ok_or_else(|| {\n        format!(\n            \"All OAuth callback ports {:?} are in use. Close any other CipherBox instances and try again.\",\n            PREFERRED_PORTS\n        )\n    })?;\n\n    let port = listener\n        .local_addr()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"Failed to get server address: {}\", e))?\n        .port();\n\n    // Generate a random nonce to validate POSTs (prevents blind injection by local processes)\n    let nonce: String = format!(\"{:016x}\", rand::rng().random::<u64>());\n    let event_name = format!(\"oauth-callback-{}\", port);\n\n    log::info!(\"OAuth callback server started on port {} (event: {})\", port, event_name);\n\n    // Spawn the server task — it will shut down after receiving the callback\n    // or after the absolute deadline (120s from now).\n    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app, event_name.clone(), nonce));\n\n    Ok(OAuthServerInfo { port, event_name })\n}"
}

You can close this window.';\n  })\n  .catch(function(err) {\n    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;\n  });\n})();\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\"#;",
  "new_string": "/// Build callback HTML with an embedded nonce for POST validation.\n///\n/// This page runs in the OAuth popup webview after Google redirects back.\n/// It extracts the token from the URL fragment (implicit flow uses fragments)\n/// and POSTs it back to the same localhost server with the nonce. The server\n/// validates the nonce before emitting the Tauri event.\nfn build_callback_html(nonce: &str) -> String {\n    format!(\n        r#\"<!doctype html>\n<html>\n<head>\n<style>body{{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}}</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<p id=\"msg\">Completing sign-in...</p>\n<script>\n(function() {{\n  var hash = window.location.hash.substring(1);\n  var params = new URLSearchParams(hash);\n  var idToken = params.get('id_token');\n  var error = params.get('error');\n  var state = params.get('state');\n\n  fetch('/token', {{\n    method: 'POST',\n    headers: {{ 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }},\n    body: JSON.stringify({{\n      id_token: idToken || null,\n      error: error || null,\n      state: state || null,\n      nonce: '{nonce}'\n    }})\n  }})\n  .then(function() {{\n    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Sign-in complete. You can close this window.';\n  }})\n  .catch(function(err) {{\n    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;\n  }});\n}})();\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\"#,\n        nonce = nonce\n    )\n}"
}

tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);\n\n    loop {\n        let accept_result = tokio::select! {\n            result = listener.accept() => result,\n            _ = tokio::time::sleep(timeout) => {\n                log::warn!(\"OAuth callback server timed out after 120s\");\n                // Emit error event so the frontend doesn't hang\n                let _ = app.emit(\"oauth-callback\",               return;\n            }\n        };\n\n        let (mut stream, _addr) = match accept_result {\n            Ok(conn) => conn,\n            Err(e) => {\n                log::error!(\"OAuth remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);\n                // Read remaining body bytes if we don't have everything yet\n                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);\n    let callback_html = build_callback_html(&expected_nonce);\n\n    loop {\n        let accept_result = tokio::select! {\n            result = listener.accept() => result,\n            _ = tokio::time::sleep_until(deadline) => {\n                log::warn!(\"OAuth callback server timed out (absolute deadline)\");\n                let _ = app.emit(&event_name, OAuthCallbackPayload {\n                    id_token: None,\n                    error: Some(\"OAuth callback server timed out\".to_string()),\n                    state: None,\n n,\n            _ => continue,\n        };\n\n        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n\n lower.trim_start_matches(\"content-length:\").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()\n                    } else {\n                        None\n                    }\n                })\n Some(header_len) = header_end {\n                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);\n                let remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);\n                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= buf.len() {\n                    let mut left = remaining;\n                    while left > 0 {\n                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {\n                            Ok(0) => break,\n                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }\n                            Err(_) => break,\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n            let body = full_request\n d,\n                Err(e) => {\n                    log::warn!(\"OAuth POST: invalid JSON body: {}\", e);\n                    send_response(&mut stream, \"400 Bad Request\", \"Invalid request\").await;\n                    continue;\n                }\n            };\n\n            // Validate nonce — reject POSTs not from the callback page we served\n            if post_data.nonce.as_deref() != Some(&expected_nonce) {\n                log::warn!(\"OAuth POST: nonce mismatch, ignoring\");\n                send_response(&mut stream, \"403 Forbidden\", \"Invalid nonce\").await;\n                continue;\n            }\n\n            let payload = OAuthCallbackPayload {\n                id_token: post_data.id_token,\n                error: post_data.error,\n                state: post_data.state,\n            };\n\n            let has_token = payload.id_token.is_some();\n            log::info!(\n                \"OAuth callback received: has_token={}, error={:?}\",\n                has_token,\n                payload.error\n            );\n\n            // Emit the port-scoped Tauri event to the main webview\n            if let Err(e) = &mut tokio::net::TcpStream,\n    status: &str,\n    body: &str,\n) {\n    let response = format!(\n        \"HTTP/1.1 {}\\r\\n\\\n         Content-Type: text/html\\r\\n\\\n         Content-Length:   "old_string": "/// Close all oauth-popup windows.\nfn close_oauth_popups(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {\n    // Popup labels are \"oauth-popup-0\", \"oauth-popup-1\", etc.\n    // Try to close a reasonable range.\n    for i in 0..100 {\n        let label = format!(\"oauth-popup-{}\", i);\n        if let Some(window) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {\n            let _ = window.close();\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Close all oauth-popup windows up to the current counter value.\nfn close_oauth_popups(app: &tauri::AppHandle) {\n    let current = POPUP_COUNTER.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n    for i in 0..current {\n        let label = format!(\"oauth-popup-{}\", i);\n        if let Some(window) = app.get_webview_window(&label) {\n            let _ = window.close();\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

POST requests must include this nonce — requests with a missing or mismatched nonce are rejected with 403 and the server keeps waiting for the real callback. Combined with the TypeScript-side state validation, this prevents both blind injection and preemptive DoS from local processes.'",
  "description": "Reply to thread #2 (server-side validation)"
}

  "description": "Reply to thread #3 (timeout reset)"
}

Receives the POST at `/token`, emits a Tauri event, and shuts down\n+///\n+/// Returns the port number so the frontend can build the redirect_uri:\n+///   `http://localhost:{port}/callback`\n+///\n+/// The server auto-terminates after 120 seconds if no callback arrives.\n+#[tauri::command]\n+pub async fn start_oauth_server(app: tauri::AppHandle) -> Result<u16, String> {\n+    // Try preferred ports first (pre-registered in Google Cloud Console)\n+    let mut listener = None;\n+    for &port in PREFERRED_PORTS {\n+        match TcpListener::bind(format!(\"127.0.0.1:{}\", port)).await {\n+            Ok(l) => {\n+                log::info!(\"OAuth callback server bound to preferred port {}\", port);\n+                listener = Some(l);\n+                break;\n+            }\n+            Err(e) => {\n+                log::debug!(\"Preferred port {} unavailable: {}\", port, e);\n+            }\n+        }\n+    }\n+\n+    // Fallback: random port\n+    let listener = match listener {\n+        Some(l) => l,\n+        None => {\n+            log::warn!(\n+                \"All preferred ports {:?} unavailable, using (120s).\n+    tokio::spawn(run_oauth_server(listener, app));\n+\n+    Ok(port)\n+}\n+\n+/// Minimal callback HTML page served by the OAuth callback server.\n+///\n+/// This page runs in the OAuth popup webview after Google redirects back.\n+/// It extracts the token from the URL fragment (implicit flow uses fragments)\n+/// and POSTs it back to the same localhost server, which then emits a Tauri event.\n+const CALLBACK_HTML: &str = r#\"<!doctype html>\n+<html>\n+<head>\n+<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>\n+</head>\n+<body>\n+<p id=\"msg\">Completing sign-in...</p>\n+<script>\n+(function() {\n+  var hash = window.location.hash.substring(1);\n+  var params = new URLSearchParams(hash);\n+  var idToken = params.get('id_token');\n+  var error = params.get('error');\n+  var state = params.get('state');\n+\n+  // POST the extracted fragment data back to the same server\n+  fetch('/token', {\n+    method: 'POST',\n+    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n+    body: JSON.stringify({\n+      id_token: idToken || null,\n+      error: error || null,\n+      state: state || null\n+    })\n+  })\n+  .then(function() {\n+    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Sign-in complete. You can close this window.';\n+  })\n+  .catch(function(err) {\n+    document.getElementById('msg').textContent = 'Error: ' + err.message;\n+  });\n+})();\n+</script>\n+</body>\n+</html>\"#;\n+\n+/// Response page shown after the token POST is received.\n+const DONE_HTML: &str = r#\"<!doctype html>\n+<html>\n+<head>\n+<style>body{background:#000;color:#006644;font-family:'JetBrains Mono',monospace;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0}</style>\n+</head>\n+<body>\n+<p>Sign-in complete. You can close this window.</p>\n+</body>\n+</html>\"#;\n+\n+/// OAuth callback data emitted via Tauri event.\n+#[derive(Clone, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]\n+struct OAuthCallbackPayload {\n+    id_token: Option<String>,\n+    error: Option<String>,\n+    state: Option<String>,\n+}\n+\n+/// Run the OAuth callback server until a token is received or timeout.\n+async fn run_oauth_server(listener: TcpListener, app: tauri::AppHandle) {\n+    let timeout = tokio::time::Duration::from_secs(120);\n+\n+    loop {\n+        let accept_result = tokio::select! {\n+            result = listener.accept() => result,\n+            _ = tokio::time::sleep(timeout) => {\n+                log::warn!(\"OAuth callback server timed out after 120s\");\n+                // Emit error event so the frontend doesn't hang\n+                let _ = app.emit(\"oauth-callback\", OAuthCallbackPayload {\n+                    id_token: None,\n+                    error: Some(\"OAuth callback server timed out\".to_string()),\n+                    state: None,\n+                });\n+                return;\n+            }\n+        };\n+\n+        let (mut stream, _addr) = match };\n+\n+        // Read the HTTP request — headers may arrive before the body,\n+        // so we may need multiple reads for POST requests.\n+        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];\n+        let mut total = 0usize;\n+        // Initial read\n+        match stream.read(&mut buf).await {\n+            Ok(n) if n > 0 => total = n,\n+            _ => continue,\n+        };\n+\n+        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n+\n+        // Parse the first line to determine method and path\n+        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n+\n+        if first_line.starts_with(\"POST /token\") {\n+            // Parse Content-Length from headers to know how much body to expect\n+            let content_length = initial_request\n+                .lines()\n+                .find_map(|line| {\n+                    let lower = line.to_ascii_lowercase();\n+                    if lower.starts_with(\"content-length:\") {\n+                        lower.trim_start_matches(\"content-length:\").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()\n+                    } else {\n+                        None\n+                    }\n+                })\n+                .unwrap_or(0);\n+\n+            // Find where headers end (\\r\\n\\r\\n) and calculate how much body we have\n+            let header_end = initial_request.find(\"\\r\\n\\r\\n\").map(|i| i + 4);\n+            if let Some(header_len) = header_end {\n+                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);\n+ remaining <= buf.len() {\n+                    let mut left = remaining;\n+                    while left > 0 {\n+                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {\n+                            Ok(0) => break,\n+                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }\n+                            Err(_) => break,\n+                        }\n+                    }\n+                }\n+            }\n+\n+            // Re-parse with full data\n+            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n+            let body = full_request\n+                .split(\"\\r\\n\\r\\n\")\n+                .nth(1)\n+                .unwrap_or(\"\");\n+\n+            let payload: OAuthCallbackPayload =\n+                serde_json::from_str(body).unwrap_or(OAuthCallbackPayload {\n+                    id_token: None,\n+                    error: Some(\"Failed to parse callback data\".to_string()),\n+                    state: None,\n+                });\n+\n+            let has_token = payload.id_token.is_some();\n+            log::info!(\n+                \"OAuth callback received: has_token={}, error={:?}\",\n+ All done. Here's the summary:

## Review Resolution Summary

| # | Comment | Verdict | Action |
|---|---------|---------|--------|
| 1 | Event scoping (global `oauth-callback`) | **Fixed** | Port-scoped event names (`oauth-callback-{port}`) in `oauth.rs` + `auth.ts` |
| 2 | Server-side POST validation | **Fixed** | Random nonce embedded in callback HTML, validated on POST (`oauth.rs`) |
| 3 | Timeout resets on each connection | **Fixed** | Absolute deadline via `Instant::now() + Duration` + `sleep_until` (`oauth.rs`) |
| 4 | Popup cleanup hardcoded to 0..99 | **Fixed** | Uses `POPUP_COUNTER.load()` instead (`oauth.rs`) |
| 5 | `core:event:default` too broad | **Not applicable** | OAuth popups load external URLs, can't access Tauri APIs |
| 6 | Random port fallback unreliable | **Fixed** | Removed fallback, fails fast with clear error message (`oauth.rs`) |

**6 threads resolved** / **5 fixes implemented** / **1 marked not applicable**

> DEVELOPER

with the latest changes, specifically to the callback ports, which exact redirect urls need to be configured in the google oauth console?

> AGENT

Based on the current code at `oauth.rs:62`:

```rust
const PREFERRED_PORTS: &[u16] = &[14200, 14201, 14202];
```

The redirect URIs to register are:

- `http://localhost:14200/callback`
- `http://localhost:14201/callback`
- `http://localhost:14202/callback`

The `/callback` path comes from `auth.ts:742`:
```typescript
const redirectUri = `http://localhost:${port}/callback`;
```

No random port fallback anymore — if all three are taken, the login fails with an explicit error rather than trying an unregistered port.

685	 * Get a Google credential (ID token) via OAuth2 implicit flow in a popup.
686	 *
687	 * GIS One Tap doesn't work in Tauri webview (no Google session, unregistered
688	 * origin). Instead, open a proper Google OAuth consent page in a popup window.
689	 *
690	 * **Redirect URI handling:**
691	 * Google OAuth rejects custom URI schemes like `tauri://localhost` (used in
692	 * production Tauri builds on macOS). To solve this, the Rust backend starts
693	 * a temporary localhost HTTP server that receives the OAuth redirect. The
694	 * callback page extracts the token from the URL fragment and POSTs it back
695	 * to the server, which emits a Tauri event to the main webview.
696	 *
697	 * In dev mode (`http://localhost:1420` origin), the localhost callback server
698	 * is still used for consistency.
699	 *
700	 * Requires `http://localhost` (with the callback server port) to be registered
701	 * as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console. Since the port is
702	 * dynamic, register `http://localhost` as an authorized JavaScript origin and
703	 * add a few specific redirect URIs or use a loopback redirect approach.
704	 *
705	 * **Google's loopback IP redirect policy:**
706	 * For desktop apps, Google allows `http://localhost:<port>/path` redirect URIs
707	 * without pre-registering each port, as long as `http://localhost` is
708	 * registered as an authorized redirect URI in the Google Cloud Console.
709	 */

234	            Err(e) => {
235	                log::error!("OAuth server accept error: {}", e);
236	                continue;
237	            }
238	        };
239	
240	        // Read the HTTP request — headers may arrive before the body,
241	        // so we may need multiple reads for POST requests.
242	        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];
243	        let mut total = 0usize;
244	        match stream.read(&mut buf).await {
245	            Ok(n) if n > 0 => total = n,
246	            _ => continue,
247	        };
248	
249	        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
250	        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
251	
252	        if first_line.starts_with("POST /token") {
253	            // Parse Content-Length to know how much body to expect
254	            let content_length = initial_request
255	                .lines()
256	                .find_map(|line| {
257	                    let lower = line.to_ascii_lowercase();
258	                    if lower.starts_with("content-length:") {
259	                        lower.trim_start_matches("content-length:").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()
260	                    } else {
261	                        None
262	                    }
263	                })
264	                .unwrap_or(0);
265	
266	            // Read remaining body bytes if headers arrived before body
267	            let header_end = initial_request.find("\r\n\r\n").map(|i| i + 4);
268	            if let Some(header_len) = header_end {
269	                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);
270	                let remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);
271	                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= buf.len() {
272	                    let mut left = remaining;
273	                    while left > 0 {
274	                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {
275	                            Ok(0) => break,
276	                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }
277	                            Err(_) => break,
278	                        }
279	                    }
280	                }
281	            }
282	
283	            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);
284	            let body = full_request
285	                .split("\r\n\r\n")
286	                .nth(1)
287	                .unwrap_or("");
288

57	## Resolution
58	
59	root_cause: In production Tauri builds, window.location.origin resolves to "tauri://localhost" (macOS custom protocol). The getGoogleCredential() function in auth.ts uses window.location.origin to construct the OAuth redirect_uri, producing "tauri://localhost/google-callback.html". Google OAuth server-side validation rejects any redirect_uri with a non-http/https scheme, returning Error 400: invalid_request.
60	
61	fix: Added a temporary localhost HTTP callback server on the Rust side (start_oauth_server command in commands/oauth.rs). The server tries preferred ports (14200, 14201, 14202) first, falling back to random. It serves a callback HTML page that extracts the OAuth fragment (#id_token=...) and POSTs it back to the same server, which emits a Tauri event (oauth-callback) to the main webview. The frontend (auth.ts) now calls start_oauth_server to get the port, uses http://localhost:PORT/callback as redirect_uri, and listens for the oauth-callback Tauri event instead of polling localStorage.
62	
63	verification: Rust cargo check passes. TypeScript tsc --noEmit passes. Awaiting human verification of end-to-end OAuth flow.
64	
65	files_changed:
66	
67	- apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/oauth.rs
68	- apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs
69	- apps/desktop/src/auth.ts
```

  "old_string": "          const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;\n\n          if (error) {\n            reject(new Error(error));\n            return;\n          }\n\n          // CSRF validation: verify state matches\n          if (returnedState !== state) {\n            reject(new Error('OAuth state mismatch (possible CSRF)'));\n            return;\n          }\n\n          if (id_token) {\n            resolve(id_token);\n          } else {\n            reject(new Error('No ID token received from Google'));\n          }",
  "new_string": "          const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;\n\n          // Validate state first — ignore events with mismatched state\n          // (prevents local processes from injecting error payloads)\n          if (returnedState !== state) {\n            console.warn('[Google Auth] Ignoring event with mismatched state');\n            return;\n          }\n\n          if (error) {\n            reject(new Error(error));\n            return;\n          }\n\n          if (id_token) {\n            resolve(id_token);\n          } else {\n            reject(new Error('No ID token received from Google'));\n          }"
}

  "new_string": "        // Read the HTTP request — headers and body may arrive in separate\n        // TCP segments, so loop-read until we have complete headers (\\r\\n\\r\\n)\n        // and then read any remaining body bytes based on Content-Length.\n        let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];\n        let mut total = 0usize;\n\n        // Phase 1: Read until we have complete headers (\\r\\n\\r\\n)\n        loop {\n            match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {\n                Ok(0) => break,\n                Ok(n) => total += n,\n                Err(_) => break,\n            };\n            let so_far = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n            if so_far.contains(\"\\r\\n\\r\\n\") {\n                break;\n            }\n            if total >= buf.len() {\n                break; // Buffer full, proceed with what we have\n            }\n        }\n        if total == 0 {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let initial_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);\n        let first_line = initial_request.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n\n        if first_line.starts_with(\"POST /token\") {\n            // Parse Content-Length from complete headers\n            let content_length = initial_request\n                .lines()\n                .find_map(|line| {\n                    let lower = line.to_ascii_lowercase();\n                    if lower.starts_with(\"content-length:\") {\n                        lower.trim_start_matches(\"content-length:\").trim().parse::<usize>().ok()\n                    } else {\n                        None\n                    }\n                })\n                .unwrap_or(0);\n\n            // Read remaining body bytes if we don't have everything yet\n            let header_end = initial_request.find(\"\\r\\n\\r\\n\").map(|i| i + 4);\n            if let Some(header_len) = header_end {\n                let body_so_far = total.saturating_sub(header_len);\n                let remaining = content_length.saturating_sub(body_so_far);\n                if remaining > 0 && total + remaining <= buf.len() {\n                    let mut left = remaining;\n                    while left > 0 {\n                        match stream.read(&mut buf[total..]).await {\n                            Ok(0) => break,\n                            Ok(n) => { total += n; left = left.saturating_sub(n); }\n                            Err(_) => break,\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            let full_request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..total]);"
}

The server tries fixed preferred ports (14200, 14201, 14202) and fails fast if none are available (no random port fallback). It serves a callback HTML page (with embedded nonce for POST validation) that extracts the OAuth fragment (#id_token=...) and POSTs it back to the same server, which validates the nonce and emits a port-scoped Tauri event (oauth-callback-{port}) to the main webview. The frontend (auth.ts) now calls start_oauth_server to get the port and event name, uses http://localhost:PORT/callback as redirect_uri, and listens for the scoped Tauri event."
}

dev mode works because origin is http://localhost:1420\n+\n+- timestamp: 2026-05-25T00:00:40Z\n+  checked: OAuth popup mechanism (commands/oauth.rs)\n+  found: Popup is a Tauri WebviewWindow loading Google OAuth URL as external URL. The redirect happens within this popup webview.\n+  implication: The popup webview can navigate to any URL but Google validates redirect_uri before granting auth\n+\n+- timestamp: 2026-05-25T00:00:45Z\n+  checked: Web app auth flow (apps/web GoogleLoginButton.tsx)\n+  found: Web app uses Google Identity Services (GIS) One Tap / native button - no redirect_uri needed. Desktop uses manual OAuth2 implicit flow with redirect_uri because GIS doesn't work in Tauri webview\n+  implication: Desktop needs different OAuth approach than web, which is why it has a redirect_uri at all\n+\n+- timestamp: 2026-05-25T00:01:00Z\n+  checked: Google OAuth documentation and RFC 8252\n+  found: Google allows http://localhost with dynamic ports for native/desktop app clients per RFC 8252. For web app clients, exact redirect URI must be pre-registered.\n+  implication: Using a fixed set of preferred ports (14200-14202) that can be pre-registered in Google Console is the safest approach\n+\n+- timestamp: 2026-05-25T00:01:30Z\n+  checked: Rust compilation (cargo check) and TypeScript compilation (tsc --noEmit)\n+  found: Both compile cleanly with the fix applied\n+  implication: Code is syntactically and type-safe correct\n+\n+## Resolution\n+\n+root_cause: In production Tauri builds, window.location.origin resolves to \"tauri://localhost\" (macOS custom protocol). The getGoogleCredential() function in auth.ts uses window.location.origin to construct the OAuth redirect_uri, producing \"tauri://localhost/google-callback.html\". Google OAuth server-side validation rejects any redirect_uri with a non-http/https scheme, returning Error 400: invalid_request.\n+\n+fix: Added a temporary localhost HTTP callback server on the Rust side (start_oauth_server command in commands/oauth.rs). The server tries preferred ports (14200, 14201, 14202) first, falling back to random. It serves a callback HTML page that extracts the OAuth fragment (#id_token=...) and POSTs it back to the same server, which emits a Tauri event (oauth-callback) to the main webview. The frontend (auth.ts) now calls start_oauth_server to get the port, uses http://localhost:PORT/callback as redirect_uri, and listens for the oauth-callback Tauri event instead of polling localStorage.","path":".planning/debug/google-oauth-tauri-redirect.md","commit_id":"5446cddfe24cb83c1754ce73b17124d1c24d9835","original_commit_id":"5446cddfe24cb83c1754ce73b17124d1c24d9835","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed: `@FSM1` Here is the review for PR `#459`.

---

## PR Review: `fix(desktop): use localhost callback server for Google OAuth in Tauri`

The overall approach is sound — using a local loopback server is the standard pattern for OAuth in native apps (RFC 8252). The nonce-in-HTML and state CSRF validation are good ideas. I found a few issues ranging from a logic bug that will cause hangs to security improvements worth making.

---

### 🔴 Critical — `auth.ts`: `cleanup()` called before state validation causes the promise to hang

In `getGoogleCredential()`, `cleanup()` is invoked as the **first** thing inside the `listen` callback, unconditionally removing the event listener. If the `state` doesn't match (e.g. a race condition or malicious local event), the promise is left unresolved and silently waits until the 2-minute timeout fires:

```typescript
// apps/desktop/src/auth.ts — current (buggy)
unlisten = await listen<{...}>(eventName, (event) => {
  cleanup();  // ← listener removed here, before state check
  
  const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;

  if (returnedState !== state) {
    console.warn('[Google Auth] Ignoring event with mismatched state');
    return;  // ← Promise hangs; the listener is already gone
  }
  // ...
});
```

`cleanup()` should only be called after the state is confirmed valid. Move it inside the success/error branches (or after the state check):

```typescript
unlisten = await listen<{...}>(eventName, (event) => {
  const { id_token, error, state: returnedState } = event.payload;

  // Validate state BEFORE cleanup — a mismatch should be ignored,
  // not treated as a terminal event.
  if (returnedState !== state) {
    console.warn('[Google Auth] Ignoring event with mismatched state');
    return; // keep listening
  }

  cleanup(); // safe to clean up now that the state is confirmed

  if (error) {
    reject(new Error(error));
    return;
  }

  if (id_token) {
    resolve(id_token);
  } else {
    reject(new Error('No ID token received from Google'));
  }
});
```

---

### 🔴 Security — `oauth.rs`: Use `OsRng` for the nonce

`thread_rng()` delegates to a PRNG seeded from the OS, but is **not** guaranteed to be cryptographically secure across all platforms/Rust versions. Since this nonce guards the `/token` endpoint against injection by local processes, it should be generated with a CSPRNG:

```rust
// Before
use rand::{thread_rng, Rng};
let nonce: String = format!("{:016x}", thread_rng().gen::<u64>());

// After
use rand::{rngs::OsRng, RngCore};
let mut nonce_bytes = [0u8; 16];
OsRng.fill_bytes(&mut nonce_bytes);
let nonce: String = hex::encode(nonce_bytes);
```

Or use the `getrandom` crate directly if `rand/OsRng` isn't already in the dependency tree.

---

### 🟡 Security — `oauth.rs`: `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` undermines the nonce

Both the `GET /callback` response (which serves the HTML **containing the nonce**) and the `POST /token` response carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. This means any local page can:
1. `fetch('http://localhost:14200/callback')` — get the HTML with the embedded nonce.
2. Parse the nonce.
3. `fetch('http://localhost:14200/token', { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify({ nonce, ... }) })` — inject an arbitrary `id_token`.

The CORS header on `GET /callback` should be removed (it's only ever opened by the browser, not cross-origin fetched), or origin-restricted to `null` (documents served from `file://` / Tauri origins):

```rust
// For GET /callback: drop Access-Control-Allow-Origin entirely,
// or restrict to the Tauri webview origins:
// Access-Control-Allow-Origin: tauri://localhost
// (or https://tauri.localhost on Windows)
```

The `OPTIONS` handler and the `POST /token` response can similarly be tightened to only allow the Tauri app origin rather than `*`.

---

### 🟡 `oauth.rs`: OAuth popup not closed on timeout or error

`close_oauth_popups` is only called on the happy path (successful token POST). If the server times out or receives an error from the OAuth provider, the popup window is left open:

```rust
// Timeout branch — emits error event but never closes popup
_ = tokio::time::sleep_until(deadline) => {
    let _ = app.emit(&event_name, OAuthCallbackPayload { error: Some("..."), .. });
    // ← close_oauth_popups(&app) missing here
    return;
}
```

```rust
// After emitting timeout error:
close_oauth_popups(&app);
return;
```

---

### 🟡 `oauth.rs`: GET handler serves callback HTML for all paths

The GET catch-all serves the callback page (with embedded nonce) to **any** GET request that isn't `POST /token` or `OPTIONS`. Browser-initiated requests for `/favicon.ico`, `/`, etc. all receive the nonce-bearing HTML. Consider gating it on path `/callback`:

```rust
} else if first_line.starts_with("GET /callback") {
    send_response(&mut stream, "200 OK", &callback_html).await;
} else {
    send_response(&mut stream, "404 Not Found", "Not found").await;
}
```

---

### 🔵 Minor — Leftover internal dev note in comment

In `run_oauth_server`, the deadline comment references a stale review note:

```rust
// Absolute deadline — connections cannot extend the server lifetime (fix: review `#3`)
```

The `(fix: review `#3`)` suffix looks like a leftover from a previous review cycle and should be removed.

---

### 🔵 Minor — `close_oauth_popups` iterates all historical popup labels

`close_oauth_popups` iterates from `0` to the current counter and calls `get_webview_window` for every label ever created. Windows from prior OAuth flows will just return `None`, so this is harmless — but it could be made cleaner by tracking only the label(s) created by `start_oauth_server` and closing specifically those.

---

### Summary table

| Severity | File | Issue |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 Critical | `auth.ts` | `cleanup()` before state check causes promise hang on mismatch |
| 🔴 Security | `oauth.rs` | `thread_rng()` not CSPRNG — use `OsRng` for nonce |
| 🟡 Security | `oauth.rs` | `ACAO: *` on GET callback lets local pages steal nonce |
| 🟡 Bug | `oauth.rs` | Popup not closed on server timeout / OAuth error |
| 🟡 Minor | `oauth.rs` | GET catch-all serves nonce HTML for any path |
| 🔵 Nit | `oauth.rs` | Stale `(fix: review `#3`)` comment |
| 🔵 Nit | `oauth.rs` | `close_oauth_popups` iterates all historical labels |
```

}

> AGENT

Done. All CodeRabbit review items addressed:

| # | Issue | Resolution |
|---|-------|-----------|
| 1 | `cleanup()` before state check causes hang | **Fixed** — moved after state validation |
| 2 | `thread_rng()` not CSPRNG | **Fixed** — switched to `OsRng` (128-bit) |
| 3 | `ACAO: *` exposes nonce | **Fixed** — removed from GET/POST responses |
| 4 | Popup not closed on timeout | **Fixed** — added `close_oauth_popups` to timeout branch |
| 5 | GET catch-all serves nonce for any path | **Fixed** — gated on `GET /callback`, 404 for others |
| 6 | Stale review comment | **Fixed** — removed |
| 7 | `close_oauth_popups` iterates all labels | **Not applicable** — harmless |
